Open intakes still feeding the queue, each with its own evidence file: the 2026-07-07 CRR / PS1/26 Art. 111–241 compliance audit (`docs/plans/compliance-audit-crr-111-241-rectification.md`, WS5–WS11 residue; three findings refuted in its §7), the 2026-06-01 conformance audit (Tier 9), the 2026-07-19 reporting-estate architecture review (Tier 6), the 2026-11-13 Basel 3.1 filing-readiness issue log, the 2026-11-16 PRA001 rule review (publisher issues PI-01 to PI-40 in `docs/rwa-filer/publisher-issues.md`) and the 2026-11-18 product & structure review (`docs/plans/product-review-2026-11-18.md`).

## Status Legend

- [ ] Not started
- [~] Partial / needs rework
- [x] Complete (pruned to the reference list at the end of this file)

**ID conventions:** `P<tier>.<n>` — items filed on this plan. `D<n>.<n>` — items migrated from the retired `DOCS_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (2026-08-08 merge), codes preserved for git-history traceability; new items always take the next free P-code. Tier 5 is the docs queue: `/next-docs` selects from Tier 5 only, every other tier belongs to `/next-items`.

## Architecture Migration (cross-reference)

> **2026-06-11:** A full architecture review produced a target architecture (regime-as-data rulepacks, producer-sealed eager stage edges, uniform stage model) and a phased (0–8) strangler migration plan: **`docs/plans/target-architecture-migration.md`**. Phases 0 and 1 landed 2026-06-11 (merged via PR #386); Phase 2 (dead-path deletion, branch-path error channel, protocol diet, sentinel-to-None) landed 2026-06-12 on `feat/phase2`. Next: Phase 3 (producer-sealed edge contracts). Plan-curator: schedule migration work per phase from that document rather than minting parallel P-codes; phase-relevant existing items are called out in its "folds in" notes (e.g. P2.21 → Phase 4; P1.183/P2.16b → Phase 4 aggregate-stage conversion; P8.39/P8.53/P5.17 CCR wirings → Phase 4 "ordinary registered stage" work; P2.5/P2.7/P2.25/P2.27/P2.28/P4.20 → Phase 7 per-template modules). The do-not-do register in that plan is binding for all batches (notably: no bulk golden regeneration; no agent edits to `tests/oracle/`; Float/String nulls never filled to 0.0).

- **P1.315** [ ] (LOW) **Art. 127(4) commercial limb of the P1.257 blend, deliberately deferred.** The Art. 127(4) flat-100% applies to the part secured "in accordance with **Article 126**", and the engine's only proxy for the Art. 126(2) qualifying test is `has_income_cover` — which **P1.263 records as carrying the INVERTED sense on the CRR branch** (the 50% CRE weight is granted when `has_income_cover=True`, the opposite of Art. 126(2)(b)). Because the blend REDUCES RWA, building the commercial limb on a flag known to be backwards would grant relief to the wrong population; gating on the CRE class alone would be broader than the article allows, also in the relieving direction. **Blocked on P1.263** — settle the flag's meaning first, then mirror the RRE blend with the Art. 126(2)(d) 50% cap (`COMMERCIAL_RE_PARAMS["ltv_threshold"]`). **Effort: S**
- **P1.259** [ ] **RE-SCOPED 2026-07-26** (batch 20260724b) — **investigated, NOT implemented; the bullet as written must not be executed.** (MED) PS1/26 Art. 138(1)(g)/139(6): no field flags a rating as incorporating implicit government support, so support-inflated bank ratings are accepted; from 1 Jan 2027 they must be excluded for institution obligors. **Effort: M→L** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 138(1)(g)/139(6) · Ev: `data/schemas.py` RATINGS_SCHEMA, `engine/stages/hierarchy/ratings.py`
  - **Regulatory claim VERIFIED** against `ps126app1.pdf` p.72: Art. 139(6) reads "An institution, when determining the risk weight of an exposure to an issue where: (a) **the obligor is an institution**; and (b) there is no issue-specific credit assessment available … that does not incorporate assumptions of implicit government support …, **shall use the higher of** … (ii) … **disregarding point (g) of Article 138(1)**." Mandatory ("shall"), institution-obligor-only, B31-only (CRR Art. 138 has (a)-(f); CRR Art. 139 has (1)-(4)).
  - **DO NOT ship the literal P2.40a→P2.40b schema-first split.** `contracts/edges.py` is *not* needed for a schema-only add (`RAW_TABLE_EDGES` is derived from the schema dicts, so it does not force single-stream) — but that is exactly the problem: the hierarchy stage selects an explicit column list and `conform` strips undeclared columns, so a schema-only field would be **unreachable**, not merely unread. That is strictly worse than P1.291's dead `is_solicited` / `rating_is_inferred`. **Hit rate on speculative rating flags in this repo is 1 of 3** — only `rating_is_issue_specific` ever got a consumer.
  - **TWO fields, at different grains, and one cannot substitute for the other.** `rating_incorporates_government_support` on RATINGS_SCHEMA (per assessment — genuinely varies by agency: Moody's BCA vs support-uplifted LT, S&P SACP vs ICR), nullable Bool with **no default** so the tri-state survives `edge_columns_from_specs`' Boolean fill; and `is_government_owned_or_sponsored` on COUNTERPARTY_SCHEMA (per counterparty, invariant across agencies), default `False` — an affirmative carve-out, absence cannot manufacture one. A single collapsed "usable" flag is **regulatorily insufficient**: Art. 139(6) requires computing candidate (ii) *from the assessment (g) disallows*, which a pre-collapsed flag cannot reconstruct.
  - **Default must be null, not True.** `default=True` would drop every rated institution exposure in every fixture and every live book to SCRA on the day the field lands. Null = "no ECAI methodology review performed" is a **governance** gap and belongs in the error channel (a DQ warning on an institution obligor under a B31 pack), not in the risk weight. Repo precedent: `is_equivalent_jurisdiction` and `is_lease_collateral_attested` both carry "no default on purpose".
  - **BLOCKER FOUND WHILE SCOPING (not in the investigation):** the consuming filter must be pack-`Feature`-gated, but **no resolved pack is threaded into this stage at all** — `ratings.py::build_rating_inheritance_lazy`, `graph.py::build_counterparty_lookup` and `resolver.py::_build_rating_inheritance_lazy` all lack a `pack` parameter. Gating therefore requires signature changes across three hierarchy modules on the highest-blast-radius path in the engine. This is the main reason the item was not landed in batch 20260724b.
  - **Recommended cut — at the REGULATORY seam, not the schema/logic one.** **P1.259 (revised):** both fields + the **Art. 138(1)(g) suppression limb only**, behind a b31-only Feature (`sa_implicit_support_ratings_disapplied`), implemented as a filter in `ratings.py` **before** the Art. 138 ranking — order matters, because 138(1)(g) removes assessments from the *selection set*, which changes which row the second-best rule picks; annotating the winner is wrong. Because suppression is pre-selection, `external_cqs` simply resolves to the clean rating or null, so **no new column crosses the hierarchy edge and `contracts/edges.py` stays untouched**. Number-neutral on the existing book (no fixture sets the flag). Plus the pack threading above.
  - **P1.259b (new, Effort L, SINGLE-STREAM):** the Art. 139(6) higher-of. Needs a second CQS carrier for candidate (ii), an aggregate for "does a clean issue-specific assessment exist" computed over the pre-selection set, and edits to four edge contracts (`CP_LOOKUP_EDGE`, `CP_LOOKUP_PARENTS_EDGE`, `crm_exit`).
  - **Scope extension the bullet misses:** under CRM substitution the exposure is weighted as an exposure to the *guarantor*, so where the guarantor is an institution 138(1)(g) bites on its rating too. `crm/guarantees.py` derives `guarantor_cqs` from the same Art. 138-resolved `cqs`, so a suppression inside `ratings.py` propagates automatically — correct, but it means guarantee-substitution numbers move as well and must be tested deliberately.
  - **Sequencing:** **P1.291 is the policy item and should run first or be folded in** — it decides "consume or delete" for the existing dead rating flags, which sets the rule for whether P1.259 may split at all. P1.261 (`rating_currency_scope`) is a third addition to the same RATINGS_SCHEMA block and will textually conflict, so keep all three in one stream. Note `docs/specifications/basel31/sa-risk-weights.md:909-926` publishes a "Not Yet Implemented — Input-Schema Gap" warning telling firms to pre-adjust `external_cqs` offline; a schema-only landing would not let that be withdrawn.
- **P1.260** [ ] **RESCOPED 2026-08-15 (audited, fully specified, NOT implemented — ready to execute). The defect reproduces, but the bullet is wrong THREE ways and its literal prescription is RWA-REDUCING.** **Reproduces end-to-end**, full `PipelineOrchestrator` over the standard fixture bundle, CRR/SA/2025-12-31 — four subordinated corporate rated rows: `LOAN_CORP_SUB_001` (CQS2) 0.5 → 1.0, `CONT_SUB_001` (CQS2) 0.5 → 1.0, `FAC_CORP_SUB_001_UNDRAWN` (CQS2) 0.5 → 1.0, `LOAN_SUB_001` (CQS3) already 1.0 and correctly not demoted. **Δ = +5,426,776.70; portfolio 385,467,495.57 → 390,894,272.27.** ⚠ **(1) THE BULLET'S LITERAL RULE IS RWA-REDUCING AND MUST NOT BE BUILT.** "Null the CQS for subordinated rows where the table RW would be lower" — but the CRR unrated **institution** weight is Art. 121(1) **sovereign-derived**, not the table's 100%. Measured, subordinated institution with `cp_sovereign_cqs=1`: unrated **20%**, CQS2-rated **50%**. The bullet's rule nulls that 50% and drops it to 20% — a **30pp cut**, where Art. 139(2)(a) requires the 50% to be **KEPT** (higher RW + junior ranking satisfies limb (a)). This is the P1.314 shape. **(2) The bullet quotes only limb (b) and omits limb (a)** — verbatim, `crr.pdf` p.idx 135: *"(a) it produces a **higher** risk weight … and the exposure ranks pari passu or **junior** …; (b) it produces a **lower** risk weight and the exposure ranks pari passu or **senior** …. In all other cases, the exposure shall be treated as unrated."* The rule is two-sided; the correct result for a subordinated row is exactly `max(rated_RW, unrated_RW)`. **(3) "CRR." is WRONG — PS1/26 Art. 139(2) SURVIVES** (`ps126app1.pdf` p.idx 70, closing `[Note: This rule corresponds to Article 139 of CRR…]`, not `[Provision left blank]`), so **no pack `Feature`** — a `crr`-only Feature would assert something false. Number-neutral under B31 by measurement (`_is_b31_subordinated_debt` already pins subordinated corporate to 150% at every CQS incl. null; B31 total **435,214,240.54 before and after**). **(4) The named edit site is wrong** — `enrich.py:105-155` precedes CRM, so nulling there would move the Art. 224 supervisory haircuts (`crm/haircuts.py:442,584`, `haircut_tables.py:308`) and `guarantor_cqs` (`crm/guarantees.py:391`). **NOT the P1.259 trap** — `seniority` is on the calc-branch edge (`contracts/edges.py:1684`), in `SA_INPUT_CONTRACT` (`risk_weights.py:162`, default `"senior"`), and is **already read inside the SA chain** at `:1577`. **SPEC:** a new module beside `engine/sa/cqs_lift.py`, invoked from `_prepare_risk_weight_lookup` **after** the `_lookup_class`/`_lookup_cqs` block (`:1063-1088`) and **before** the join at `:1089`. Predicate `is_subordinated & (_lookup_class == "CORPORATE") & (rated_table_rw < unrated_table_rw)` → `cqs → null`, `_lookup_cqs → -1`; both weights from two joins of the already-resolved 14-row `rw_table`, **no hard-coded CQS numbers** (arch_check 5/6). ⚠ **CORPORATE-only, and strict `<`**: the gate is sound only where the table's unrated row lower-bounds the row's true unrated weight — corporate unrated is `max(100%, sovereign RW)` so `rated < 100% ⟹ rated < unrated` always, whereas institution unrated ranges 20%-150% and the same gate cuts capital. Equality is neither limb. Record two residuals in the docstring: institutions out of scope, and CQS3-corporate-in-a-CQS6-sovereign (100% should be 150%) left as a safe under-fix. ⚠ **`engine_fill_null_sites` = 468 sets a trap whose obvious workaround is the graduated Attack-8 failure**: `risk_weights.py:1566-1578` exists to keep the `seniority.fill_null` site singular, but `_is_b31_subordinated_debt` returns a **composite** (`subordinated AND (INSTITUTION OR CORPORATE)`) — reusing it silently imports the INSTITUTION limb, i.e. exactly the RWA-reducing scope. **Extract a bare `_is_subordinated()` shared by both.** `max_engine_module_loc` 1709 vs `risk_weights.py` 1689 — new module, not inline. **FIXTURE (5 legs, ratings must be `rating_type="external"` or `cqs` is null and the fixture is inert):** corporate/subordinated/CQS2 **moves** 50% → 100%; corporate/subordinated/CQS**5** **survives** at 150% (limb (a) — rating higher than unrated ⇒ KEEP); corporate/subordinated/CQS3 **survives** at 100% (strict `<`, not `<=`); **institution/subordinated/CQS2 with `cp_sovereign_cqs=1` stays 50% — the LEAK DETECTOR, which fails at 20% under the bullet's rule** (C1.11 mutation evidence); corporate/**senior**/CQS2 stays 50% (no seniority spill). **No defect-pinning tests** — the moving rows appear in no golden and no acceptance assertion; `test_high_risk_ignores_seniority` is a near-miss that stays green. ⚠ `tests/acceptance/stress/` generates 8-10% subordinated rows but **every assertion is RELATIVE** and passes through both the correct fix and the unsafe one — not a gate here. **Reporting: nothing moves** — no `reporting_*` portfolio has a subordinated row, `sa_cqs` is never sealed, and Pillar 3 CR5 splits on `external_cqs`, untouched. **Adjacent, do NOT fold in:** `ratings.py` inherits an ultimate parent's rating into `cqs`, which PS1/26 Art. 139(4) prohibits outright — separate bullet. **Direction: RWA-INCREASING (+5.4m measured), no sign-off needed. Whoever takes P1.288b must confirm the IRB side or explicitly scope it out.

**WS9 — SA retail & real-estate** (files: `risk_weights.py`, `enrich.py`, `attributes.py`, `flagging.py`, `schemas.py`)
- **P1.262** [ ] (MED) PS1/26 Art. 124E — a RE exposure is materially-dependent by DEFAULT (with enumerated exceptions), but the engine defaults `is_income_producing` to False → the preferential 20% loan-split instead of the 30-105% whole-loan tables. - **P1.263** [ ] (MED) CRR Art. 126 — the 50% CRE weight is granted when `has_income_cover=True` — the OPPOSITE of Art. 126(2)(b) (preferential only where risk does NOT materially depend on the property). Split the semantics; the same column carries inverted meaning across regimes. CRR. **Effort: M** | Ref: CRR Art. 126 · Ev: `risk_weights.py:782-788`, `re_split/flagging.py:157-160` · §5 WS9
- **P1.292** [ ] **RESCOPED 2026-08-13 (LOW→MED, GOLDENS-MOVING) — the diagnosis survives intact; the scope does not. ⚠ NEEDS OPERATOR SIGN-OFF for a surgical golden regeneration before implementation.** The first bullet in this batch whose premise, direction and liveness all held up.
  - **REGULATION CONFIRMED.** PS1/26 Art. 124A(1) (`ps126app1.pdf` p.50) frames qualification as a conjunctive test — *"A real estate exposure is a regulatory real estate exposure if it is not an ADC exposure and **all the following requirements are met**"* — whose limbs include firm-side attestations: (b) legal enforceability assessed in all relevant jurisdictions, (d) *"the value of the property is determined in accordance with Article 124D"*, (f) *"the institution has in place procedures to monitor that the property is adequately insured"*. Those are not properties discoverable from an exposure record. An unreported assessment means the firm has **not established** qualification, so unknown must mean non-qualifying.
  - **MEASURED AND LIVE.** B31 residential, LTV 0.50, EAD 100,000: `is_qualifying_re` True → **0.20**, **NULL → 0.20**, False → **0.75**. `risk_weights.py:586` is `fill_null(True)` with the rationale recorded only as *"backward compatible"* (`:591`). No upstream default rescues it — the opposite: `stages/hierarchy/enrich.py:764` **deliberately creates the column as a typed null** and `:387-389` states *"null means 'unreported' and is treated as qualifying here"*. **55pp anti-conservative; the fix is RWA-INCREASING**, so no reduction sign-off is needed.
  - **PRECEDENT IS ALREADY SET, AND THIS CASE IS THE MORE SERIOUS ONE.** `test_null_qualifies_as_retail_is_non_qualifying` records the identical flip for the sibling flag on 2026-06-12: *"the 75% weight is preferential … unknown qualification must not receive it"*. The Art. 124F 20% band is preferential in exactly that way. **But note the asymmetry:** for `qualifies_as_retail` the null case was **defensive only** — the classifier always emits non-null, so that fix moved no production number. Here the pipeline *deliberately emits null*, so this one is live. No recorded decision points the other way; "backward compatible" is not a regulatory argument.
  - ⚠ **SCOPE — the bullet names two sites; there are FIVE, and one of its two is the wrong column.** `re_split/flagging.py:165` is `re_collateral_non_qualifying`, a different column with its own (correct) `fill_null(False)` — not a site of this defect. The five that must move together: `engine/sa/risk_weights.py:586` (the capital number), `engine/stages/hierarchy/enrich.py:389` (the per-beneficiary Art. 124J gate, which the bullet misses entirely), `reporting/corep/c07.py:738`, `reporting/corep/c09.py:523`, and `engine/stages/re_split/splitter.py:423-426` (which preserves the parent's null onto split legs). **Flipping the engine without the two COREP sites makes reporting silently disagree with capital on exactly the rows that moved.**
  - ⚠ **NOT EFFORT S — IT MOVES THE MAIN B31 GOLDEN.** Only six fixtures set `is_qualifying_re` at all; every other RE-bearing fixture leaves it null and currently takes preferential weights. **`tests/fixtures/reporting_portfolio.py` — the main golden portfolio — does not set it, and its B31 CR5 real-estate rows carry 10,400,000 of exposure.** Those weights move. Per `.claude/LESSONS.md` E1 the regeneration must be surgical (scoped to one `test_reporting_*_golden.py`, since `REGEN_REPORTING_GOLDENS` is read per golden file) with a per-cell preserve-or-fix record written **before** asking.
  - **FAIL-FIRST TEST:** B31, `exposure_class="residential_mortgage"`, `property_type="residential"`, `ltv=0.50`, `is_qualifying_re=None`, natural person, EAD 100,000. Today RW **0.20** / RWA **20,000**; must assert RW **0.75** / RWA **75,000** (Art. 124J). **Add the CRR mirror asserting the CRR RE path is UNMOVED** — CRR has no Art. 124A, so a leak across regimes is the obvious way this fix goes wrong.
  - Keep the bullet's DQ-warning half: an unreported Art. 124A assessment on an RE row is a reportable data-quality condition. **Effort: M, goldens-moving** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 124A(1)(b)/(d)/(f), Art. 124J
  - ⚠ **ATTEMPTED 2026-08-13 AND REVERTED — BLOCKED ON P1.353, NOT ON THE GOLDEN REGENERATION.** All five sites were changed and the capital fix verified (a purpose-built test moved null 0.20 → 0.75 and reddened on revert, with explicit-True/explicit-False/CRR controls holding). But the reporting estate then produced **11 failures including 6 NEW supervisory cross-template breaks** (`boe_b0978`-`boe_b0983`, all WARNING, none Error-severity), and the cause is a **second, previously invisible defect** rather than the expected golden movement — see **P1.353**. Reverted rather than regenerated: banking goldens while C 07.00 and C 09.01 disagree about the same population would have made an internal inconsistency permanent, and the regeneration is operator-gated precisely so that decision is not taken by accident. **The capital half is correct and ready; it cannot ship until P1.353 settles the C 07.00 row definitions.**

Nothing else changes: the five sites, the fail-first test and the ⚠ **surgical, operator-signed golden regeneration** all still stand, and **P1.378** shares that regeneration.

#### Tier 1 — follow-ups raised by the 2026-07-24 plan drain (batch plan-drain-2026-07-24)

> Registered by the orchestrator while draining P1.248/249/251/252/253/276/277/278/279/281. Each was found by an implementer or a reviewer, deliberately scoped out of its parent item, and is recorded here rather than lost. Severity in brackets; where a fix would REDUCE RWA it is flagged, because those need arbitration rather than drive-by implementation.
>
> **Process finding worth acting on first (Effort: S, high leverage):** four separate pre-existing tests/fixtures in that batch pinned a premise the audit had refuted, and every one was detectable from the words **"uniform"** or **"all classes"** in a test name or docstring (e.g. `test_crr_uniform_floor_all_classes_equal`, which asserted the very thing P1.277 removes). Two of the four passed *anyway* because their assertions were relative rather than absolute, so they silently protected the defect. **Grep `tests/` for those two phrases and audit each hit against its article.**
>
> **Extended 2026-08-13 (P1.333 test wave) — add `"backward compat"` to the grep list, and add a second, harder shape.** P1.333's single defect-pinning test was `TestBackwardCompatibility::test_missing_columns_backward_compat`, asserting an unfloored 40% on a row the Art. 121(6) floor should have raised to 100%. **The class name was doing the concealing:** "backward compatibility" frames an unfloored weight as a deliberate legacy accommodation, so a reader sees an intentional carve-out rather than a capital shortfall, and the accompanying prose ("only a frame that omits the column entirely … is left unfloored") asserted an absent-column asymmetry that measurement then refuted — `ensure_columns` fills both columns, and the 40% was pure P1.333. Renamed to `TestNullAndMissingSovereignInputs`. **The harder shape, found in the same sweep and not caught by any phrase grep:** `test_uk_institution_usd_floor_applies` has a docstring asserting "FX, sovereign floor applies" while the floor was in fact abstaining — the 40% assertion survived either way because a CQS 1 jurisdiction's floor is 0% and cannot bind. That is a test whose **docstring claims a behaviour its assertion is structurally incapable of detecting**, which no keyword search finds. The generalisable check is to ask, of any test naming a rule, *what value would this assertion take if the rule never ran at all* — if the answer is "the same", the test does not cover the rule it names.

Same for the sovereign-treated guarantor labels (named MDB, `rgla_sovereign`, `pse_sovereign`), whose SA-class label does not distinguish them from institution-treated siblings. **Conservative (over-floors), so fixing it REDUCES RWA — needs arbitration.** Note the repo's Art. 118 IO list is separately wrong (P1.283), so widening on that label would compound a known defect. **Effort: S** | Ev: `engine/irb/formulas.py::_pd_floor_expression` ladder

- **P1.348** [ ] **RESCOPED 2026-08-13 (audited, then implementation ATTEMPTED AND REVERTED — read this block before the original text below).** The core premise survives: an A-IRB-permitted row with a null LGD really is demoted, and under an A-IRB-only permission map it really does reach `standardised`. **But three of the bullet's four load-bearing claims are wrong, and the fix it prescribes is the wrong fix.**
  - **The 60-65% headline is a wrong-baseline artifact.** It compares an F-IRB result *with* collateral relief against an A-IRB result *without* it. Like-for-like, the understatement is **400,000.00 vs 460,158.33 = 13.1%**, not 60-65%. Still a real understatement; an order of magnitude smaller, and the item's priority should be set from 13.1%.
  - **The article is wrong.** The SA fall-through is governed by **Art. 149(1)** (IRB → SA), not Art. 149(2), which governs the A-IRB → F-IRB reversion. The bullet's whole "prohibited without prior competent-authority permission" framing is attached to the wrong limb.
  - **The prescribed fix is wrong, and its blast radius is understated.** "Remove the conjunct here" moves **~30 golden rows** across `rich`, `irb-classes` **and `crm-substitution`** — the third portfolio the bullet does not mention. The correct fix is narrower: keep `has_modelled_lgd` on `airb_expr`, and route only `model_airb_permitted & ~has_modelled_lgd & ~model_firb_permitted` to F-IRB instead of letting it fall to SA.
  - **Attempted, measured, reverted.** The audit measured the narrow fix at **zero** moved rows across the 7 registered portfolios × both regimes — but that measurement is **scoped to registered reporting portfolios and does not cover acceptance fixtures**, where it is *not* zero. Applying it produced **2 failures + 18 errors**:
    - `tests/unit/test_classifier.py::TestModelPermissions::test_airb_permission_without_lgd_falls_to_sa` — `assert 'foundation_irb' == 'standardised'`. **A test that pins the defect in its own name** (8th instance of this pattern in this batch). It must be inverted, not satisfied.
    - `tests/acceptance/basel31/test_p1_248_art_161_5_partially_secured_lgd_floor.py` — 18 errors: five `F-L-*_UNDRAWN` legs newly enter `irb_results`. **Whether an undrawn commitment leg legitimately belongs on F-IRB under Art. 149(1) is unresolved** and is the real open question on this item.
  - **Retail is deliberately out of scope.** Art. 151(9) makes retail A-IRB-only, so there is no F-IRB rung to fall back to — an unsettled design question, not an omission to close here.
  - **Sequencing:** fixing this creates the retail dilution population P1.305 describes — assert that cell holds at 0.75 (CRR) / 1.00 (B31).
  **Effort: M, and the P1.248 undrawn-leg question must be arbitrated first.** Original text follows.

  **(original, 2026-08-13, found by the P1.299 refutation — the SAME predicate, opposite sign, and this one is live)** — **The MODEL-permission path silently reverts an A-IRB-permitted row to Foundation, or to Standardised, on a null LGD — understating capital 60-65% on collateralised exposures.** `engine/stages/classify/permissions.py:~340,~345` conjoins `has_modelled_lgd` into `airb_expr`, so a row whose firm holds an A-IRB permission but whose `lgd` is null falls through to F-IRB — or, under an **A-IRB-only** permission map, all the way to Standardised. Measured on a GB corporate, EAD 1,000,000, PD 2%, plus 600,000 eligible cash: A-IRB+F-IRB permissions → `foundation_irb`, `rwa_final` **460,010.54**; A-IRB-only permission → `standardised`, **400,000.00**; against the correct A-IRB treatment at **1,150,026.35**. Understatement of **60%** and **65%** respectively. **This is the reversion CRR Art. 149(2) forbids without prior competent-authority permission and a demonstration that it is not proposed to reduce own funds** — here it reduces them by more than half. Unlike P1.299 this path IS live in the default `permission_mode="irb"` configuration and IS reachable from the public API. **Direction: RWA-INCREASING to fix, so it needs arbitration, not a drive-by** — and it touches the `irb-classes` and `rich` golden portfolios. **The correct fix is not to add the missing conjunct to the sibling; it is to remove it here**, and to route the null to the error channel (BR001 already fires) rather than to a lower-capital approach. **Effort: M** | Ref: CRR Art. 149(2), Art. 151(8)/(9); PS1/26 Art. 150(1) rule para 4(b), Art. 151(7)(b) · Ev: `engine/stages/classify/permissions.py:~340,~345`; `stages/classify/approach.py:114`

That is the gate working, not a fixture bug — but it means the pinning fixture must be designed around it.

- **P5.57** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-15, trap found by the P1.310 audit) — `OWNER: P5.57`. A fragment with EMPTY `entries` auto-absorbs divergent pack entries, so routine work silently restages a read-only skill file.** `scripts/generate_regulatory_tables.py` has exactly one such fragment — `.claude/skills/basel31/references/what-changed.md` / `regime-divergence` — and `_render_fragment_body` falls back to `_divergent_names(packs)` for it. That region therefore **auto-absorbs every simple-shape entry present in both packs whose values differ**. Consequence: adding any new CRR-off/B31-on `Feature` — a routine pattern in this repo — restages a file that is **read-only in some environments**, blocking the item with no obvious cause. (A non-divergent, identical Feature is safe.) This has now blocked or part-blocked five items: P1.296, P1.267, P1.334, P1.302, P1.310. **Fix options, in order:** (a) make the fragment's membership explicit rather than computed, so a new Feature does not silently join it; (b) have `--check` report *which entry* newly entered a fragment, so the cause is visible; (c) make `.claude/skills/` writable in the sandbox. **Effort: S for (b), M for (a)** | Ref: `scripts/generate_regulatory_tables.py::_render_fragment_body`, `_divergent_names`

- **P1.316** [~] **RESCOPED 2026-08-08** (batch 20260808-1624) — **design complete and reviewed; dropped at the Wave-1 gate on a guard gap.   - **DIRECTION IS MIS-FILED.** Not an understatement — **net RWA-REDUCING**. CQS 1 goes 100%→20% (−80pp) and CQS 2 100%→50% (−50pp), which dominate any real institution book; only CQS 6 rises (100%→150%). Correct characterisation: *conservative at CQS 1-2, coincidentally correct at CQS 3-5, anti-conservative at CQS 6.*

The shape every existing sovereign-floor test uses (`local_currency="TRY", currency="USD"`) is **green before the fix**.

The floor the bullet calls "CRR Art. 121(6)" is **PS1/26** Art. 121(6) (`ps126app1.pdf` p.42), applied unconditionally to both regimes at `risk_weights.py:377`. Five comment/docstring sites misattribute it.

Implement as an **RW-valued** expression (no CQS lift — that is the only shape that trips the `cp_sovereign_cqs` Int32 → `cqs` Int8 trap), placed **after** the Art. 121(3) ≤3m branch.

Both trade-finance guards were pinned at 0.5y, inside the 1-year window, so neither can see it. **Refile must add a guard at `residual = original = 5.0y` expecting 1.00.**

Keep `.eq_missing(True)` anyway (ratchet-safe, house style, null-safe if the producer changes) but do not repeat the "`~pl.col` would revert all six rows" claim. (b) A defaulted unrated institution does **not** re-blend from the ladder — `_crr_defaulted_re_secured_share` returns NULL for a non-RE class so Art. 127 overwrites `risk_weight` outright from `provision_rw`; measured identical at CQS 1/2/6.

#### Tier 1 — follow-ups raised by batch 20260808-1624 (P1.316 / P1.318 / P1.319)

> Five defects surfaced by the Wave-0/Wave-1 adversarial gate that were **not on the plan**. Three were found while auditing a bullet that turned out to be wrong about something else. Direction is flagged on each; the RWA-reducing ones need arbitration, not drive-by implementation.

- **P1.323b** [~] **SAME DEFECT AS P1.333 — close together, and read P1.333's REFUTATION before attempting either.** Its prescribed fix (`default=False` at `eu_sovereign.py:77`) is doubly wrong: the dict is `str → str`, so Polars silently coerces `False` to the **string `'false'`** (measured: `default=None → ['EUR', None, None]`; `default=False → ['EUR', 'false', 'false']`), meaning it would only ever "work" by accident of truthiness — and the semantics it aims at were refuted by the oracle in the 2026-08-13 attempt, because treating an unknown domestic currency as *foreign* floors every non-EU institution transacting in its own currency. Its one durable contribution is the consumer census: `risk_weights.py`'s negated use is the **only** negated consumer in `src/`, verified independently, with every other consumer taking the positive form where NULL already falls to the conservative branch. Its claimed measurement "`local_currency=None, country=DE, currency=USD` → 1.50 = what a fixed `default=False` looks like" is **not evidence of anything** — DE *is* in the EU map, so `replace_strict` returns `"EUR"`, `.eq("USD")` is a deterministic False, and the `default=None` path is never taken; that row behaves identically before and after. *Original bullet text follows for provenance:* (**MED — ANTI-CONSERVATIVE, silent**) **`engine/eu_sovereign.py:74-79` uses `replace_strict(_EU_COUNTRY_DOMESTIC_CURRENCY, default=None)`, so `is_eu_domestic` is NULL — not False — for every non-EU country.** Then `is_domestic_currency = is_uk_domestic | is_eu_domestic` = `False | NULL` = **NULL**, `~NULL` = NULL, and `_floor_applies` at `risk_weights.py:1514` nulls out so `pl.when(NULL)` takes the `otherwise` branch. Net: **the PS1/26 Art. 121(6) sovereign floor silently never fires for any non-UK/non-EU institution row lacking `cp_local_currency`, in BOTH regimes including the B31 SCRA arm.** Measured in both directions: `local_currency=None, country=US` → 1.00 (the defect suppressing the floor); `local_currency=None, country=DE, currency=USD` → 1.50 (what a fixed `default=False` looks like). **Precisely scoped:** `risk_weights.py:1499` is the **only negated use** in `src/` — every other consumer (`stages/classify/approach.py:304`, `irb/guarantee.py:329`, `crm/guarantees.py:584`, `sa/rw_adjustments.py:787`, `risk_weights.py:1070/1266`) uses the positive form, where NULL falls through to the conservative branch. So the item is a narrow fix at `eu_sovereign.py:77` / `risk_weights.py:1499`, not a broad `is_eu_domestic` rework. Found while auditing P1.316; independently confirmed by a second agent. **Understatement. Effort: S**
- **P1.324** [ ] **RESCOPED 2026-08-15 (LOW → MED) — ⚠ NEEDS OPERATOR SIGN-OFF. The bullet's direction is WRONG: measured, its prescribed fix is RWA-REDUCING, not a conservative over-statement. And its prescribed fix SITE would silently disable a HIGHER floor.**
  - **DIRECTION, MEASURED not reasoned** (`apply_irb_formulas`, EAD 500,000, M 5y; anchored against a full `PipelineOrchestrator` run of a real commercial-only-secured row, byte-identical at 281,994.63 B31 / 298,914.31 CRR). Δ = RWA as `retail_other` minus RWA as `retail_mortgage`, i.e. the effect of the prescribed fix: PD 0.05% **−38.0%** (B31) / +6.4% (CRR); PD 0.10% +4.4%; **crossover at PD ≈ 0.229%**; PD 1% **−18.8%**; PD 3% **−43.9%**; PD 10% **−63.0%**, both regimes. The Art. 154(1) retail-other curve exceeds 0.15 only *below* PD ≈ 0.229%, so across the whole of a realistic secured retail book the bullet's fix **releases capital**. The 10% RWEA floor never acts as the increasing limb — at these PDs modelled RW is 56%+ and `mortgage_rw_floor_adjustment` measured **0.0**. The single RWA-increasing limb is the LGD floor, and only where own-LGD sits below it (40%-covered, own LGD 3%: 0.100 → 0.220, +115%…+130%).
  - ⚠ **THE PRESCRIBED FIX SITE IS WRONG — do not move the class.** Three independently sufficient reasons: **(1)** Art. 154(3) is **not** residential-confined in either regime — PS1/26 p.104 and CRR p.151 both read *"For retail exposures secured by **immovable property collateral** a coefficient of correlation (R) of 0.15…"*, so commercial-secured retail is genuinely entitled to 0.15 and the class is the engine's only route to it (`formulas.py:777-778`, `:1065`). **(2)** CRR Art. 164(4) carries an explicit **commercial** limb that is **HIGHER** than the residential one (`crr.pdf` p.160: *"…secured by residential property … not lower than **10%**. …secured by **commercial immovable property** … not lower than **15%**"*), implemented at `engine/aggregator/_lgd_floor_check.py:87-99` over a population bucketed by `property_type == "commercial"` **within** `retail_mortgage` — narrowing the class **silently empties that bucket and the 15% floor stops firing**. **(3)** Blast radius: `re_split/flagging.py:198-209` *excludes* `RETAIL_MORTGAGE` from the loan splitter, so reclassified rows become newly splitter-eligible (changing the SA leg, which per LESSONS D1 feeds the B31 output floor), and `reporting/pillar3/templates.py:833-840` defines the CR9 leaves `retail_cre_sme` / `retail_cre_non_sme` as `CR9ClassSpec(("retail_mortgage",), property_type="commercial")`, which narrowing makes structurally unreachable.
  - **CORRECTED PREMISE — B31-only, three surgical gates, class untouched.** The defect is not an over-inclusive class; it is that three **B31 residential-limbed** consequences read the class instead of a residential-collateral carrier. `residential_collateral_value` is **already on `IRB_BRANCH_EDGE`** — no schema or edge work. (Prefer it to `property_type`, a single joined scalar at `enrich.py:618` that is unreliable for multi-collateral rows.) **(A)** Art. 154(4A)(b) 10% RWEA floor, `engine/irb/adjustments.py:235-237` — add `residential_collateral_value > 0` to the gate; **RWA-REDUCING**; leave the UK limb alone (ORC-142 / P1.337) and keep the `:232-233` comment recording it as unimplementable. **(B)** Art. 163(1)(b) PD floor, `engine/irb/formulas.py:194-195` — `MORTGAGE|RESIDENTIAL` → 0.001 where commercial-only-secured is owed Art. 163(1)(c) 0.0005; **RWA-REDUCING**; CRR untouched (flat 0.03%). **(C)** Art. 164(4)(a)/(c) LGD floor, `formulas.py:242-250`, `:290-306`, and the blend deny-list at `:520` — not within (a) ⇒ owed the (c) LGD\* blend (LGDU 30% / LGDS 10% immovable); measured at 40% CRE coverage the engine gives **0.100** against a correct **0.220**; **RWA-INCREASING**, the only limb that adds capital.
  - **Verbatim, and NARROWER than the bullet says** — PS1/26 p.104 Art. 154(4A)(b): *"…retail exposures secured by **UK residential** immovable property…"*; Art. 147(5B) p.90: *"…secured by **residential** immovable property"*; Art. 163(1)(b) p.114: *"0.1% for retail exposures secured by residential immovable property **located in the UK**"*.
  - **Chain corrections:** every cited line range has drifted — real sites are `enrich.py:374-482` (docstring `:384-385`), `attributes.py:633-647`, `subtypes.py:211-219` — and the bullet **misses a second mapping site**, `subtypes.py:531-545` (corporate→retail reclassification via `_build_has_property_expr` at `:684-700`).
  - **Defect-pinning:** `tests/unit/test_corporate_to_retail_reclassification.py:524` and `:807` both **absolute**, both would correctly block a class-level change, both **pass unchanged** under the corrected premise. `tests/unit/test_irb_post_model_adjustments.py:670` needs its docstring corrected — it calls this residual "conservative", which the measurement refutes.
  - ⚠ **UNOBSERVABLE TODAY.** All seven registered reporting portfolios × both regimes: commercial-only-secured retail = **0** in every one. Needs a new fixture registered in `RUNS` **and** its builder in `generate_all.py`. Shape: GB natural person + term loan + one `collateral_type="real_estate"`, `property_type="commercial"`, `is_eligible_irb_collateral=True`, and a `model_permissions` row with `exposure_class="retail_mortgage"` (an `="retail"` permission does **not** match — routes to SA with CLS006). Two-leg: a residential sibling that must not move, plus a **partially**-covered low-own-LGD variant so limb (C)'s increase is exercised (a fully-covered row leaves the LGD floor inert, measured). **Effort: M, RWA-REDUCING on two of three limbs, needs sign-off** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 154(4A)(b), 147(5B), 163(1)(b)/(c), 164(4); CRR Art. 154(3), 164(4)
- **P1.362** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-15, found by the P1.324 audit; deliberately NOT folded in)** — **The SA real-estate branch appears insensitive to property kind for a retail-secured row.** Measured: a commercial-only-secured retail row takes SA RW **0.35** (CRR) / **0.4475** (B31) *identically* whether `property_type` is `"commercial"` or `"residential"`. If the SA RE preferential weights are residential-limbed, that is a material understatement against the 75% retail weight. **Stated as an unverified magnitude, deliberately**: the auditor could not build a clean counterfactual, because removing the collateral also removes the CRM relief, so the delta cannot be isolated by that route. - **P1.355** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-15, batch 20260815-1, residual left standing by P1.320)** — **The QRRE obligor aggregate still over-counts a multi-option facility across the root/sub grain.** P1.320 fixed the **leg-multiplication** grain (each leg of one facility contributing the facility's limit once per row). It did **not** fix the second grain: drawn legs key on the **sub**-facility (`facility_limit` joined at `engine/stages/hierarchy/enrich.py:713`) while the root's waterfall legs key on the **root** (`facility_undrawn.py:561`), so a sub-limit is counted alongside the root limit that already contains it. Measured on real `HierarchyResolver` output — one individual, one 50,000 MOF root, two 30,000 committed subs, 10,000 drawn under `SUBA`: **current 130,000 → post-P1.320 80,000, where the economic aggregate nominal is 50,000.** **Conservative** (an over-count denies QRRE, so it is RWA-increasing) which is why it was not folded into P1.320 — a fix here is RWA-REDUCING and needs its own evidence and sign-off. Note the trap recorded in the P1.320 design §2.1: `root_facility_reference` keying is **not** the obvious answer — legs under one root carry different limits, so root-keying needs an intra-root aggregation, and `max` silently discards every sub-limit, which under-counts (RWA-reducing) whenever the root limit is null or understated, and no validator enforces `sum(sub limits) ≤ root limit`. Any fix must state which of those it assumes. - **P1.360** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-15, the capital half of P1.352) — Art. 134(7)'s `t` has NO production input, so the `1/t` residual-lease branch is degenerate at 100%.** Verbatim, CRR Art. 134(7) (`crr.pdf` PAGE_INDEX 131) and PS1/26 Art. 134(7) (`ps126app1.pdf` PAGE_INDEX 68, word-for-word identical, `[Note: This rule corresponds to Article 134 of CRR…]` — the provision survives): *"When the exposure is a residual value of leased assets, the risk-weighted exposure amounts shall be calculated as follows: **1/t \* 100 % \* residual value**, where t is the greater of 1 and the nearest number of whole years of the lease remaining."* No exposure-side raw table can carry that term. **Fix:** add a **lease-scoped** `lease_remaining_term_years` (`ColumnSpec(pl.Float64, required=False)`) to `LOAN_SCHEMA` (beside the lease-convention comment at `data/schemas.py:637-653`) and `RAW_TABLE_EDGES["loans"]`, add it to `SA_INPUT_CONTRACT` so it injects as a typed null when absent, and have `_leased_residual_rw_expr` read it — the null path keeps today's conservative 100%, so `test_residual_lease_null_maturity_defaults_to_hundred` stays green and stays correct. ⚠⚠ **DO NOT reuse or globally populate `residual_maturity_years`, and do not derive it from `(maturity_date − reporting_date)` mirroring `risk_weights.py:997-1010`.** It has TWO other consumers, with OPPOSITE signs, both currently inert because the column is constant-null: (i) `risk_weights.py:853`, the CRR Art. 120(2) Table 4 rated-institution short-term gate — populating it newly grants preferential weights to every rated institution with < 3 months *remaining* but a longer *original* term, **RWA-REDUCING across the whole SA institution book**, and per LESSONS D1 the SA pipeline runs unconditionally to feed the Basel 3.1 output floor, so it lowers the floor for IRB books too; (ii) `engine/crm/simple_method.py:317,507,730-745`, the Art. 239(1) FCSM maturity-mismatch gate which zeroes collateral when `coll_residual < exp_residual` — populating it newly **zeroes collateral, RWA-INCREASING**, on a path whose `fcsm` portfolio is deliberately *not* in `RUNS` and so would land completely unobserved. **Direction: RWA-REDUCING as a capability** (a correct 5-year lease is 20% against today's 100%, an 80pp reduction on the residual value) but **number-neutral on today's estate**, since no feed can supply the field. ⚠ The loader seal strips the column silently — verified, a loans frame carrying `residual_maturity_years=2.6` reached the pipeline without it and `result.errors` was empty, so "nobody has reported this" is not evidence. Resolve the in-tree docs conflict in the same change: `docs/data-model/input-schemas.md:114` says `other_residual_lease` → "100% RW" (today's degenerate behaviour) while `:366-367` says "1/t × 100%" (the rule). **Effort: M** | Ref: CRR Art. 134(7); PS1/26 Art. 134(7) · Ev: `engine/sa/risk_weights.py:165,464-497`; `data/schemas.py:637-653`; `contracts/edges.py:1769-1782`
- **P1.361** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-15, the coverage half of P1.352) — BLOCKED ON P1.360.** Art. 134(7) has zero production reachability (0 `other_residual_lease` rows across 28 registered runs) and its entire gate is one unit file. Build the fixture **after** the carrier lands, or it asserts the degenerate 100%. **Two-leg, with the values derived**: `t = max(1, ceil(y − 0.5))`, `RW = 1/t`, against the pre-P1.289 `1/max(1, y)`. **SURVIVOR** `y = 5.0`, residual value 4,000,000 → `ceil(4.5) = 5`, RW 20%, RWA **800,000** under both formulas — proves the column is populated, since a null would give 100%, and keeps the cell live afterwards. **MOVER** `y = 1.6`, residual value 2,000,000 → RW **50%** / RWA 1,000,000 post-fix, 62.5% / 1,250,000 pre-fix, **100% / 2,000,000 if the carrier is unwired** — three distinguishable states, which is why 1.6 is chosen over the larger-delta 1.49 (whose post-fix RW is the degenerate 100%). Optional third leg `y = 2.5` pins the tie direction (50% post, 40% pre, 33.33% under round-ties-up). **ZERO-GOLDEN-MOVEMENT HOME:** a NEW `tests/fixtures/reporting_other_items_portfolio.py` registered in `RUNS`, following the `re-split` / `crm-substitution` / `art199` precedent of a registered portfolio with no `tests/expected_outputs/reporting/<name>/` directory. Perturbing `rich` (46 + 51 golden frames) or `sa-classes` (18 + 21) would be operator-gated regeneration for **zero** coverage gain, since the cell census ratchets `(template, column)` and a new *row* lights no new column — `rich` already emits the other-items sheet. Use `_sa_config` with no IRB permission (an IRB permission routes the residual leg away from `apply_risk_weights`) and **state that in the module docstring** — this is exactly the "registered, wrong config, dead cell" third form of LESSONS B5. Registration: `RUNS` (mandatory); `template_cell_coverage_baseline.json` **only if** a column moves dead→live; `validation_known_breaks.json` if breaks/vacuity change; **NOT** `generate_all.py` — reporting portfolios are code-built DataFrames, not parquet. *Correction to a widely-repeated belief, verified in this audit:* a `RUNS` addition does **not** hard-exit the cell census on run count — `EXPECTED_RUNS` is computed from `len(RUNS)` at import (`check_template_cell_coverage.py:304`) so it self-satisfies, and `--check` diffs only the `live_columns`/`dead_columns` sets; the `counts`/`provenance` blocks are informational. **Effort: S after P1.360** | Ref: CRR Art. 134(7); PS1/26 Art. 134(7)
- **P1.351** [~] **PARTLY LANDED 2026-08-15 — fixture + pinning tests SHIPPED; the IRB009 error channel is BLOCKED on the branch-census matrix** (batch 20260815-1). **Regulatory half CONFIRMED verbatim:** CRR Art. 161(1)(e)/(f) (`crr.pdf` p.157) confine the senior/subordinated supervisory rates to purchased **corporate** receivables, and Art. 164(1) (p.160, retail sub-section) supplies a retail rate for **dilution risk only**. Under PS1/26 the confinement is **doubled** — Art. 161 is headed *"CORPORATES AND INSTITUTIONS"* **and** para 1 opens *"An institution **using the Foundation IRB Approach**"*, with Art. 161(2)(a) carrying (e)/(f) to A-IRB for that same population only. **Code half is NARROWER than filed, in three ways.** (i) `transforms.py:226-228`'s `supervisory_subtype_applies` has no exposure-class gate — contrast its PD-side sibling at `subtypes.py:455-457`, which carries the corporate gate deliberately. (ii) **CRR-demonstrable only**: no B31 retail IRB row with a null LGD could be constructed by any route (Art. 147A blocks retail F-IRB; the A-IRB gate at `permissions.py:340` requires `has_modelled_lgd`; the remaining B31 retail limbs are mutually exclusive with an IRB route). (iii) ⚠ **For `senior` the LGD is IDENTICAL either way — 0.45 with the subtype and 0.45 without it.** Measured: `is_firb_cleared` in `apply_firb_lgd` has **no subtype condition**, so any null-LGD F-IRB row clears via the seniority fallback. So the defect is **an unrecorded number, not a wrong one** — the subtype's only observable effect on this route is the audit trail. ⚠ **The audit's "it suppresses BR001" framing holds only on the org-wide A-IRB route, not this one**, and `UNKNOWN_FALLBACK` is banked in `scripts/branch_census_baseline.json` as **desired-dead** ("rows newly landing on UNKNOWN_FALLBACK are a loss of justification"), so a fixture aimed at it would be lighting a limb the estate wants dark. ⚠ **The alternative "add a class gate" fix is RWA-REDUCING and must not be taken** — measured CRR subordinated 136,590.29 → 61,465.63 (−55%), B31 212,823.78 → 85,129.51 (−60%), because the gate drops the row to the senior-unsecured fallback. **SHIPPED:** `tests/fixtures/p1_351/` (5 rows, registered in `generate_all.py`) and `tests/acceptance/crr/test_p1_351_retail_supervisory_subtype.py` (13 tests). This is **the only thing in the estate that exercises the `supervisory_subtype` dispatch end to end** — the census banks it dead because the subtype is null in every other fixture. The tests are a **fail-first anchor held in advance**: when IRB009 ships, the error assertions invert for the retail senior/subordinated rows and must stay unchanged for the dilution row (Art. 164(1) legislates for it) and the corporate row (Art. 161(1)(e) authorises it). That asymmetry is the whole guard. ⚠ **BLOCKER for the remaining half, and it is structural:** `scripts/check_branch_census.py` walks a **fixed 28-run matrix** imported from `RUNS` + `CORPUS` — it does **not** walk `tests/fixtures/`. So a standalone fixture, however registered, cannot retire the `dead` entry. Adding `IrbLgdReason.SUPERVISORY_SUBTYPE_UNAUTHORISED_CLASS` would put a **new** census id in `dead`, which `--update-baseline` **refuses** (shrink-only), leaving the ratchet permanently red. **Unblock options, cheapest first:** (a) add a subtype-bearing retail row to a **`CORPUS`** portfolio — corpus portfolios have **no golden directory**, so no golden moves, but `ExposureSpec` has no subtype field and needs threading through `tests/properties/`; (b) add one to `tests/fixtures/reporting_portfolio.py` — moves goldens across every template that portfolio feeds, operator-gated (LESSONS E1); (c) emit IRB009 without a new branch-reason member, avoiding the census entirely. **Spec for the remaining half, ready to execute once unblocked:** `IRB009` / `ERROR_UNAUTHORISED_SUPERVISORY_SUBTYPE_LGD`, `WARNING` (the row produces a conservative-or-neutral number; BR001 is WARNING for the documented reason that an ERROR on a known-open defect gets switched off rather than fixed), `ErrorCategory.DATA_QUALITY`, trigger `subtype.is_in(["senior","subordinated"]) & exposure_class_irb.is_in([...retail...]) & lgd.is_null() & ccr_modelled_lgd.is_null()` — ⚠ **an ALLOWLIST, never `!= "dilution_risk"`**, which would be the P1.354 false-positive shape; emit by extending `validate_branch_reasons`, which is **already wired** at `engine/pipeline.py:532`, so no edit to that forced-single-stream file is needed. **Effort: S once unblocked** | Ref: CRR Art. 161(1)(e)/(f), Art. 164(1); PS1/26 Art. 161(1)(e)/(f), Art. 164(1)(b) · *Original bullet retained above in substance.*
- **P1.350** [ ] **RESCOPED 2026-08-15 — ⚠ NEEDS OPERATOR SIGN-OFF (RWA-REDUCING). The rule reading is RIGHT; the bullet's reason for parking it is REFUTED by measurement.** Verbatim, PS1/26 Art. 123 (`ps126app1.pdf` p.idx 46-47): *"**3.** Subject to paragraph 4, retail exposures shall be assigned the following risk weights: (a) regulatory retail exposures that are transactor exposures shall be assigned a risk weight of 45%; …"* and *"**4.** Retail exposures arising due to loans granted … against the unconditional transfer of part of the borrower's pension or salary … shall be assigned a risk weight of 35%, provided that all the following conditions are met: (a)…(d)"*. So paragraph 4 overrides the **whole** of the paragraph-3 ladder including the 45%. The engine appends the transactor limb first (`engine/sa/risk_weights.py:603-614`). **The bullet's "population looks negligible / more likely a data error / the right answer may be a validation error" is WRONG on all three counts:** `is_qrre_transactor` is **not gated on `is_revolving` anywhere upstream** — it is a free-standing optional Boolean on `FACILITY_SCHEMA:501` with no cross-field constraint and no validator mentioning it; the classifier gates the QRRE *class* on `is_revolving` but the risk-weight limb keys on the **flag**, deliberately (P1.293). **Two reachable paths, and the second needs no odd input at all:** (A) a facility row carrying both flags, whose `_UNDRAWN` child carries both directly; **(B) `engine/stages/hierarchy/enrich.py:695` coalesces the facility's `is_qrre_transactor` onto EVERY child exposure unconditionally**, so a payroll term loan mapped under a credit-card facility inherits it without anyone ever setting both flags on one input row. **Measured, full `PipelineOrchestrator`, natural-person obligor, payroll flag on the LOAN only: CRR → 0.35, Basel 3.1 → 0.45, schema-valid, zero errors.** Regime-inconsistent on identical input, and B31 is the wrong one. So the re-ordering is **not dead code** and **a validation error would be wrong** — Art. 123(4) grants the 35% to any retail exposure meeting (a)-(d), so a firm attesting both is making a claim the rule recognises. **Scope is NARROWER than the bullet:** payroll already outranks the (b) 75% and (c) 100% limbs, so exactly **one cell per retail class** moves — `(transactor=T, payroll=T, qualifies=T)` 0.45 → 0.35; the other 21 cells are unchanged and CRR is unchanged in all 24. **SPEC, ready to execute on sign-off:** move the payroll `.when()/.then()` pair to be the **first** limb of `_b31_append_retail_branches`; **do NOT** conjoin it with `qualifies` (Art. 123(4) says "Retail exposures", not "regulatory retail" — adding it would break the already-correct `(T,T,F) = 0.35` cell and raise it to 100%); **do NOT** touch `_crr_append_retail_branches` (CRR has no "subject to" chapeau and no transactor limb at all); **do NOT** add a validation error. Reachability in the estate is **ZERO** (no fixture sets `is_payroll_loan` on a facility), so **no golden should move — if one does, stop, something other than this cell changed.** Defect-pinning: `tests/unit/test_b31_sa_risk_weights.py:3835` `test_payroll_loan_qrre_transactor_gets_45pct` is **the LESSONS C1 shape exactly** — it states the code's own ordering as its justification and cites **no article** for the precedence, i.e. test and code written from the same sentence; its assertion is absolute so it reddens rather than passing through, and must be inverted deliberately. (The bullet's `Ev:` pointer to `:3723-3724` drifted; the real test is at 3835-3859.) Ratchet note: `risk_weights.py` is at 1689 against a 1709 cap — 20 lines, comments count. **Effort: S, but RWA-REDUCING and it lowers the SA-equivalent RW feeding the B31 output floor** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 123(3) chapeau, Art. 123(4)
- **P1.365** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-15, adjacent finding from the P1.350 audit — points the OTHER way and is the larger population) — the payroll 35% limb verifies NONE of Art. 123(4)'s four conditions, in either regime.** Any row with `is_payroll_loan=True` takes 35% regardless of (a) the unconditional deduction authority, (b) the death/incapacity/unemployment insurance cover, (c) the 20%-of-net-monthly-salary aggregate cap, or (d) the **maximum original maturity of 10 years**. Limb (d) is **machine-checkable today** from `original_maturity_years` — and the oracle already knows it matters: `tests/oracle/derivations/sa_b31.py:425` (ORC-064) sets it to 8.0 precisely so the case qualifies, and **nothing reads it**. **Direction: RWA-INCREASING to close** (a non-qualifying payroll row should take 75%/100%, not 35%), and the population is larger than P1.350's single cell. **Pairing this with P1.350 is the sensible bundle** — it makes the package net-conservative rather than a standalone capital release, which is the form most likely to be signed off. (a)-(c) need attestation inputs that do not exist; (d) needs none. **Effort: S for limb (d) alone, M with the attestation inputs** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 123(4)(a)-(d); CRR Art. 123 payroll limb · Ev: `engine/sa/risk_weights.py` payroll branch, both regimes
- **P5.59** [ ] **NEW (2026-08-15, from the P1.350 audit)** — stale citations in `tests/unit/test_b31_sa_risk_weights.py`: the section banner (~:3813) reads `PAYROLL / PENSION LOAN — 35% (PRA PS1/26 Art. 123(3)(a-b))` and the `TestB31PayrollPensionLoan` docstring (~:3757) repeats it. Payroll is **Art. 123(4)**, not 123(3)(a-b). These survived because `tests/contracts/test_crr_art123_payroll_citation.py`, which bans exactly that string, scopes `src/` only. **Consider widening that contract test to `tests/` in the same change** — the gate exists and simply does not look here. **Effort: S, number-neutral** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 123(4)
- **P1.349** [~] **AMENDED 2026-09-10 — LARGELY SATISFIED ALREADY, AND THE REASON TWO ITEMS CALLED THE ARTEFACT MISSING IS A GIT-IGNORE, NOT AN ABSENT FILE.** Original ask: acquire the published template layouts (Annex II / Annex XX / the DPM export) into `docs/assets/`, because **P1.331** could not settle the C 02.00 / OF 02.00 row addresses (*"neither the Annex I / CA2 workbook nor a DPM row-ID export is in the tree"*) and **D4.80** could not settle whether UKB CR5 slot `z` is published as 300% (the instruction PDFs give column *ranges*; the band *labels* live only in the template xlsx — `.claude/LESSONS.md` E4). **VERIFIED PRESENT 2026-09-10 via openpyxl:** `docs/assets/CRR - corep-own-funds.xlsx` **is** the Annex I COREP workbook (one sheet per template — `Index`, `1`, `2`, `3`, … `35.3`; *"Effective from 1 January 2022"*). Sheet `2` is *C 02.00 - OWN FUNDS REQUIREMENTS (CA2)* with a `Rows | Item | Label` column trio: 0010 = item `1.` TOTAL RISK EXPOSURE AMOUNT, **0040 = `1.1` RISK WEIGHTED EXPOSURE AMOUNTS FOR CREDIT, COUNTERPARTY CREDIT AND DILUTION RISK**, 0050 = `1.1.1` Standardised approach, 0060 = `1.1.1.1` SA exposure classes, 0070-0211 the SA class leaves, 0240 = `1.1.2` IRB, 0250 = `1.1.2.1` *"IRB approaches when neither own estimates of LGD nor Conversion Factors are used"*, 0310 = `1.1.2.2` the own-estimates limb. Sheet `7` is C 07.00, with **row 0040 = *"of which: Secured by mortgages on immovable property - Residential property"*** and memorandum rows 0290-0320. And `docs/assets/0F07 - annex-i-of-07-00-credit-risk-sa-reporting-template.xlsx` is the published **OF 07.00** layout (plus `OF0801`/`OF0802` for C 08.01/08.02). **WHY IT READ AS MISSING:** `docs/assets/` is git-ignored (`.gitignore:9` `*.xlsx`, `:18` `docs/assets/*.PDF`), so the assets live only in the operator's main checkout — a **git worktree contains none of them**, and an agent listing `docs/assets/` from inside its worktree sees an empty directory. Read them by absolute path into the main checkout, as the PDF-extraction note already says for the PDFs. **CONSEQUENCES:** (a) **unnecessary for P1.353** — both halves of that item are now settled from the two artefacts above (see its amendment); (b) **P1.331's named blocker is satisfied for its CRR half** — sheet `2` confirms P1.331's own row analysis line-for-line, so whoever picks it up must re-test the blocker against that file before treating it as blocked; (c) **still genuinely needed** for the PS1/26 Annex I **OF 02.00** workbook (P1.331's B31 half — only the *instruction* text is in the tree, `ps1-26-annex-ii-reporting-instructions.pdf` §1.3) and for the **Annex XX disclosure** template xlsx (**D4.80**'s UKB CR5 band labels — `docs/assets/` holds *reporting* templates only; there is no disclosure-template workbook). **Effort: S (acquire the two remaining artefacts) + M (then close P1.331 and D4.80)** | Ref: `.claude/LESSONS.md` E4; P1.331; D4.80; P1.353 · Ev: `docs/assets/CRR - corep-own-funds.xlsx` sheets `2` / `7`; `docs/assets/0F07 - annex-i-of-07-00-credit-risk-sa-reporting-template.xlsx`; `.gitignore:9,18`

#### Tier 1 — follow-ups raised by batch `facility-share-fanout-20260905` (facility-share riskiest-member fan-out)

> Out-of-scope findings surfaced by the five waves of the facility-share fan-out and **deliberately not folded into it**. Provenance for each is `docs/plans/facility-share-riskiest-member.md` §10 (the design doc's own out-of-scope list) plus the wave reports under `.claude/state/fs1-gate-w*/`. Every claim below was re-verified against the cited file and, where regulatory, against `ps126app1.pdf` via pymupdf on 2026-09-05.

- **P1.367** [ ] **NEW (2026-09-05, batch `facility-share-fanout-20260905`, design doc §10.1) — ⚠ RWA-REDUCING. The output floor is taken over the modelled subset, not over the entity's totals.** `apply_floor_with_impact` builds U-TREA and S-TREA by summing only rows whose `approach_applied` is in `FLOOR_ELIGIBLE_APPROACHES` (`engine/aggregator/_floor.py:203-225`), then re-adds the SA and equity books **outside** the max (`:326` — `total_rwa_post_floor = floored_modelled_rwa + sa_rwa_total + equity_rwa_total`). PS1/26 Art. 92(2A) takes the max over the entity's totals and **both legs are whole-firm by construction**: paragraph 3 sums points (a)-(f) *"in respect of all the business activities of an institution"*, and paragraph 3A is *"the sum of points (a) to (f) of paragraph 3 … calculated without using … the IRB Approach"*. So the SA book sits on **both** sides of the article's max and cancels only at `x = 1`; the comment at `_floor.py:192-193` (*"SA exposures cancel out"*) states that identity without its condition. Consequence: wherever the floor binds the engine reports TREA above the article by `(1 − x) · SA_total`, and the floor binds more often than it should. **Conservative today — which is exactly why this is not a tidy-up: the fix RELEASES capital.** It needs its own measurement across every registered portfolio in both floor states, operator sign-off, and a deliberate re-bank of every reporting golden and of the supervisory register, all of which currently record the engine's formula. ⚠ **Sequence with P1.212**, the same subset error one level down (the GCRA cap base inside OF-ADJ) pointing the other way — measured separately, each moves the other's denominator. **Effort: L** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 92(2A), 92(3), 92(3A) (`ps126app1.pdf` p.idx 12-14) · Ev: `src/rwa_calc/engine/aggregator/_floor.py:192-193`, `:203-225`, `:326` · **2026-11-13 — now VISIBLE on the reporting surface.** OF 02.00 took its published cells: column 0010 is un-floored and row 0010 column 0030 states `x · S-TREA + OF-ADJ` over the whole book. On `b31/facility-share-binding` the template's TREA is max(1,245,491.29, 1,643,200.00) and the engine's post-floor total (OV1 row 29) is 1,699,200.00 — the 56,000 is this bullet. The tie-out `total_rwea_c02_vs_ov1` fires there, `reporting/corep/c02.py` logs the difference, and that log line is banked as a caveat in `scripts/template_cell_coverage_baseline.json` under this owner. Closing this bullet is what removes both. · **2026-11-16 — COREP no longer reads `rwa_final` for RWEA** (`dd267d3`, P1.369; `c07.py` in `6e8b73d`): every OF 07–09 / C 08.04 / C 34 RWEA cell reads `reporting.kernel.own_rwea`, so this bullet's re-bank, when it lands, shrinks to Pillar 3 (OV1, CR6–CR10, CMS1/2 — P3.8), the summaries, and the `c02.py` log line.
- **P1.368** [ ] **NEW (2026-09-05, found by the fixture wave, design doc §10.4) — the Art. 110A due-diligence input never reaches the SA branch: the override is dead code and its warning fires on every Basel 3.1 run.** `due_diligence_performed` and `due_diligence_override_rw` are accepted at the loader (`data/schemas.py:680-681` LOAN_SCHEMA, `:833-834` CONTINGENTS_SCHEMA) but are declared on **no edge contract** — `due_diligence` does not appear anywhere in `contracts/edges.py` — and `EdgeContract.conform` emits only declared columns (`edges.py:336-341`), so both are dropped before classification with **no error and no warning** (LESSONS B1). Two consequences in `engine/sa/rw_adjustments.py::apply_due_diligence_override`: **(a)** the guard at `:599` tests column **presence**, not value, so **SA004** (`contracts/errors.py:312`) is appended once on every Basel 3.1 run whatever the input says — a saturated alarm carries no information (LESSONS B9), and the FS-1 tests already filter it by code; **(b)** the early return at `:618` fires unconditionally, so the override branch at `:621-631` is unreachable from any input. **Direction: RWA-INCREASING or neutral, never reducing** — `override_applies` requires `due_diligence_override_rw > risk_weight` (`:621-623`), so the override can only raise a weight; that makes this one of the few RWA-affecting fixes that needs no capital-release sign-off. Fix = declare both columns on `_hierarchy_resolved_columns` (`edges.py:649`) and every edge down to the SA branch, re-point the SA004 guard at the **value**, and add an end-to-end test that a populated override reaches `risk_weight`. ⚠ Scope the warning while you are there: Art. 110A(5) disapplies the obligation for exposures in scope of Art. 112(1)(a)-(c), Art. 117(2) and Art. 118(1), so a portfolio-wide SA004 is over-broad even once it reads the value. **Effort: M** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 110A(2)/(4)(b)/(5) (`ps126app1.pdf` p.idx 28) · Ev: `src/rwa_calc/data/schemas.py:680-681`, `:833-834`; `src/rwa_calc/contracts/edges.py:336-341`, `:649`; `src/rwa_calc/engine/sa/rw_adjustments.py:599`, `:618-631`
- **P1.370** [ ] **NEW (2026-09-05, out-of-scope finding from the follow-up test wave) — `entity_types_by_sa_class`'s pack parameter is dead in production; its only `src/` consumer reads a constant frozen to the default pack.** `engine/entity_class_maps.py:95` binds `ENTITY_TYPES_BY_SA_CLASS = entity_types_by_sa_class()` at import with **no pack**, so it resolves through `_DEFAULT_PACK` (`:66`). The single production consumer is the CRR Art. 140(1) short-term-ECAI obligor-class gate, which builds its eligible-entity list from that constant (`engine/hierarchy/enrich.py:41`, `:251-252`) and therefore never sees the run's regime. This is the **residual of P1.311**, which threaded the two *forward* maps properly — `entity_type_to_sa_class(pack)` / `entity_type_to_irb_class(pack)` are called with the run's pack at `engine/classify/attributes.py:360-361` — and left the inverse index behind. **Number-neutral today** for the reason P1.311 measured (the two resolved packs agree exactly), so severity does not rise: it is a latent trap that arms itself the day a `packs/b31.py` override lands, which P1.303 D4 records as pending. ⚠ **Scope note the bullet must carry:** the enclosing function `apply_short_term_rating_override` (`enrich.py:166-171`) takes no `pack` argument today, so "thread the pack" is a signature change plus its call site, not a one-liner — the cheaper alternative is to delete the parameter and document the freeze. The only pack-aware caller is `tests/unit/test_p1_311_entity_class_maps_regime.py:168`, written as a forward guard, and it guards either shape. **Effort: S** | Ref: CRR Art. 140(1) · Ev: `src/rwa_calc/engine/entity_class_maps.py:66`, `:95`; `src/rwa_calc/engine/hierarchy/enrich.py:166-171`, `:251-252`

#### Tier 1 — 2026-09-08 C 07.00 / OF 07.00 Art. 112(1) class-axis review

> Out-of-scope findings from the review that produced `templates.C07_00_SA_SHEET_MAP` (the C 07.00 / OF 07.00 sheet axis re-keyed onto Art. 112(1)). The axis fix itself and three wrong `ExposureClass` docstring citations landed with that change and are **not** filed. Siblings in Tier 3: **P2.53** (row 0040 inert), **P2.54** (no CIU class). Every claim was re-verified against the cited file and, where regulatory, against `ps126app1.pdf` / `ps1-26-annex-ii-reporting-instructions.pdf` / `crr-annex-ii-reporting-instructins.pdf` via pymupdf on 2026-09-08.

- **P1.377** [~] **BASEL 3.1 HALF FIXED 2026-11-16** (`f35eedc`, lane X): OF 07.00 rows 0300 / 0320 are reported on the sheet of the class the obligor had before default (new sealed column `reporting_class_pre_default`), on the published sheets 0001–0004 and 0007–0009, held to the taxonomy both ways by `tests/contracts/test_of07_published_cells.py`; `c07_00|cell_not_reportable` 5 → 0. ⚠ **TWO READINGS BELOW ARE REVERSED for Basel 3.1:** (1) the figure on the 'in default' sheet is NOT required — table OF07.00.01.03 has no sheet-0011 cell, and the Total's data point is that figure; (2) the real-estate sheet is NOT fileable — known-issues log #27 (19.10.2026): report real-estate-origin defaults "in the total sheet s0001 ... but do not report such exposures separately ... until the missing sheet 0010 is provided" (PI-30). A defaulted high-risk leg no longer enters rows 0300 / 0320 under Basel 3.1. **Still open: the CRR half** (C 07.00 rows 0290–0320, and the three `known_vacuous_rules` entries `v0011_h`, `v0012_h`, `v2040_s`). Follow-ups: P5.80, P2.93, P2.94. **Original bullet follows.** **NEW (2026-09-10, C 07.00 row-scope audit) — the memorandum rows 0290/0300/0310/0320 are keyed on the APPLIED exposure class, so they publish on the sheets Annex II ¶52 is *about* and on NONE of the six/seven obligor-class sheets ¶54 requires.** ¶54 does not key them on the applied class: *"Exposures shall be reported in these rows where the obligors **would have been** reported in the exposure classes … **if those exposures were not assigned to the exposure classes 'in default' or 'secured by immovable property'**"* (`crr-annex-ii-reporting-instructins.pdf` PAGE_INDEX 79, printed p.80). ¶52 gives the sheet list — *"Those memorandum items shall **only** be reported for the following exposure classes"*: central governments or central banks, RGLA, PSE, institutions, corporates, retail, i.e. Art. 112(1) **(a)(b)(c)(f)(g)(h)** (PAGE_INDEX 78, printed p.79). The repo keys them on data terms alone — `_MEMO_RE_SECURED` = `("property_type", "commercial"|"residential")` and `_MEMO_DEFAULTED_RW` = `("c07_defaulted", True)` + RW band (`c07.py:398-399`, consumed at `:1397-1400`) — with **no sheet term**, so a memo row lights on whichever sheet happens to carry the leg. **MEASURED, every populated memo cell in the estate, both regimes:** `crr/rich real_estate r0290 10,000,000` · `r0310 400,000` · `crr/re-split real_estate r0290 2,000,000` · `r0310 3,700,000` · `crr/rich defaulted r0320 1,000,000` · `b31/rich defaulted r0320 1,000,000` — **nothing on institutions, corporates, retail, CGCB, RGLA or PSE on any portfolio.** **CORROBORATED by the rule set, independently of the instruction:** six live ERROR-severity rules are sheet-scoped to exactly `{0001, 0002, 0003, 0004, 0007, 0008, 0009}` = Total + ¶52's list — `v0011_h`, `v3707_s`, `v3708_s` on rows 0290/0310 (table `C 07.00.c`) and `v0012_h`, `v2040_s`, `v3709_s` on rows 0300/0320 (`C 07.00.d`) — against `validations/scope.py:160-167`, where `0010` = Art. 112(1)(i) and `0011` = (j). **THIS IS THE MISSING ATTRIBUTION FOR THREE ERROR-SEVERITY REGISTER ENTRIES, so the fix has a free red-before-green gate:** `v0011_h`, `v0012_h` and `v2040_s` sit in `known_vacuous_rules` (`tests/expected_outputs/reporting/validation_known_breaks.json:3700`, `:3722`, `:4023`) reading *"unattributed - needs investigation … every operand is EMITTED BUT UNREPORTED (null) - 96 cell(s) measured … the layout has the cell and nothing writes to it"*, sampled on `C 07.00.c[institution] r0290` and `C 07.00.d[institution] r0300`. Those three entries must leave the register when this lands. ⚠ **TWO CORRECTIONS THE AUDIT MADE — do not implement the simpler version.** (1) **The `defaulted`-sheet figure is REQUIRED, not a defect.** ¶55 of *both* Annex IIs: *"… that information shall be reported in … row 0320 **in the total and in the exposure class 'in default'**. If this exposure, before it defaulted, was an exposure to an institution, then that information shall **also** be reported in row 0320 of exposure class 'institutions'"* — the published example puts the figure on the in-default sheet **and** on the obligor's sheet, and only the second is missing. (2) **The B31 and CRR scopes genuinely differ.** PS1/26 ¶52 lists **seven** classes, adding **(g) real estate, Art. 112(1)(i)** (`ps1-26-annex-ii-reporting-instructions.pdf` PAGE_INDEX 77, printed p.78), and its ¶54 names 'Real estate' among the would-have-been classes while drawing the population from *'in default'* **only**; CRR ¶52 lists six and excludes (i), and CRR ¶54 draws from *'in default'* **or** *'secured by immovable property'*. B31 also publishes only rows **0300/0320** (no 0290/0310 — the published OF 07.00 memo block is the two default rows, and `templates.py:461` already drops the other two correctly). So under CRR the `real_estate` r0290/r0310 publication is out of scope, while under B31 the `real_estate` sheet is **in** scope for 0300/0320. **SO THE ITEM HAS TWO HALVES AND ONLY ONE OF THEM TOUCHES EVERY ROW.** (i) **Omission** — all four rows under CRR, and 0300/0320 under B31, are absent from every one of ¶54's obligor sheets. That is the whole of the derivation work and all three register entries. (ii) **Misplacement** — only **CRR 0290/0310 on `real_estate`** sit on a sheet the instruction excludes. **`defaulted` r0300/r0320 are CORRECT today** (¶55 names that sheet explicitly), and under B31 `real_estate` is in scope for 0300/0320, so **nothing is removed from the `defaulted` sheet and nothing is removed from the B31 `real_estate` sheet** — a uniform "re-key all four rows off the applied class" would delete two correct figures. **WHY IT IS ITS OWN ITEM AND NOT PART OF P2.53:** ¶54 needs *the class the obligor would have had*, and the sealed ledger carries only the applied class — closing it needs a new derivation (`cp_entity_type` is sealed and `data/schemas.py` already maps entity type → SA class, so it is reachable) **plus** a cross-sheet population, because one exposure must appear on a sheet other than its own class total — the substitution-inflow shape `reporting/corep/c07.py::c07_plans` already implements for inflows. That moves goldens on up to six sheets per portfolio. **SIBLING QUESTION, not asserted here:** ¶55 also requires the figure *"in the total"*, and `c07_plans` builds one `SheetPlan` per exposure class with **no Total sheet at all**, so every z0001-scoped rule is `sheet_not_emitted` — check that against P2.1 before widening this item. **Effort: L, single-stream (goldens-moving, shared reporting projection)** | Ref: COREP Annex II ¶51-55 (`crr-annex-ii-reporting-instructins.pdf` PAGE_INDEX 78-79); PS1/26 Annex II ¶51-55 (`ps1-26-annex-ii-reporting-instructions.pdf` PAGE_INDEX 77-78); Annex I C 07.00 memo rows (`docs/assets/CRR - corep-own-funds.xlsx` sheet `7`) · Ev: `src/rwa_calc/reporting/corep/c07.py:398-399`, `:1397-1400`; `src/rwa_calc/reporting/validations/scope.py:160-167`; `tests/expected_outputs/reporting/validation_known_breaks.json:3700,3722,4023`

P1.330 made this **strictly less reachable** — every row its Art. 197 gate removes previously contributed `fcsm_rw` to the blend and now contributes nothing, so *ineligible* collateral can no longer raise a low-RW obligor — but the gap survives for **eligible** collateral. **Art. 222(3) read literally prescribes no cap** ("shall be at least 20%", with no ceiling), so this may be correct as written and the item may close as a documented decision rather than a fix; what is not defensible is that it is currently neither decided nor recorded.
