- **P1.333** [~] **ATTEMPTED AND REVERTED 2026-08-13 — the prescribed fix is REFUTED BY THE ORACLE. Do not re-attempt `fill_null(False)`.** The one-line fix all three filings prescribe (`is_domestic_currency = (is_uk_domestic | is_eu_domestic).fill_null(False)`) was implemented, passed its own purpose-built test and the whole unit tree, **and broke three oracle cases** — ORC-052, ORC-053, ORC-055 (`tests/oracle/`, the human-reviewed golden referee). Reverting that single file returned the oracle to 211 passed, so attribution is exact.
  - **WHY IT IS WRONG.** `fill_null(False)` collapses *"the obligor's domestic currency is unknown"* into *"the exposure is foreign"*. The domestic-currency map knows only `GB → GBP` and the EU members → `EUR`; it does **not** know that USD is the domestic currency of the US. So every non-EU institution transacting **in its own currency** was newly floored. The oracle's institution cases use `{"country_code": "US", "currency": "USD"}` — a US bank in USD, which is *domestic* — and correctly expect the Art. 121(2) Table 5 SCRA weights (Grade A 40%, B 75%). The fix raised them to 100%. Note the oracle helper is named `_FOREIGN`, meaning *foreign to the UK reporting entity*, not *foreign currency* — that naming is what makes the shape easy to misread.
  - **MEASURED, three shapes, Basel 3.1, SCRA Grade A, unrated sovereign:**

    | shape | `risk_weight` | branch reason |
    |---|---|---|
    | US/USD, `cp_local_currency` **absent** (the oracle shape) | **1.00** ← the defect the fix introduces | `floor_bound` |
    | US/USD, `cp_local_currency="USD"` (explicitly domestic) | **0.40** ← already correct today | `domestic_currency` |
    | US/EUR, `cp_local_currency="USD"` (genuinely foreign) | 1.00 ← already correct today | `floor_bound` |

    Row 2 is decisive: **the engine already gets this right whenever the data is present.** The gap is not a missing `fill_null`, it is a missing input.
  - **THE PREMISE "SILENTLY DEAD" IS ALSO WRONG.** The Kleene null is a defensible encoding of *"the FX test is undecidable on this row"*, and it is **not silent** — since the Phase 3 branch-reason work those rows are named `UNKNOWN_FALLBACK` and carry a `BR001` warning, and `scripts/branch_census_baseline.json` pins the limb. The three-state distinction (known-domestic / known-foreign / **undecidable**) is real regulatory content, and any fix that collapses it to two states will be wrong on one of them.
  - **CORRECT DIRECTIONS, neither of which is Effort S.** (a) **Supply the missing data** — a cited jurisdiction → domestic-currency table in the rulepack, extending the UK/EU map to the jurisdictions the book actually holds. This is a regulatory-data question (what *is* the domestic currency of each jurisdiction, and does the PRA define it) and belongs in a pack with a `Citation`, not in engine code. It makes rows 1 and 2 of the table above agree. (b) **Require the input** — treat an unknown domestic currency on an unrated institution exposure as a **data-quality error** (the error channel, per the project's error-accumulation rule) rather than silently choosing a risk weight in either direction. (a) and (b) compose: supply what is knowable, and refuse to guess the rest.
  - **DO NOT** re-file this as "add `fill_null(False)`", and do not use P1.323b's `default=False` at `eu_sovereign.py:77` either — the dict is `str → str` and Polars silently coerces `False` to the **string `'false'`**, so it would "work" by accident of truthiness and still mis-classify the same population.
  - **P1.312 IS REFUTED and closes here** (see its own bullet): the absent-column asymmetry it describes does not exist — `ensure_columns` fills `cp_sovereign_cqs` and `cp_local_currency` from `SA_INPUT_CONTRACT`, measured on the exact frame it cites. **P1.323b remains open and is the same defect as this bullet**; close them together.
  - **P1.334 stays open and is now MORE urgent, not less.** It records that UK CRR Art. 121 has four paragraphs and contains no (5) or (6), so the floor should not apply under CRR at all. Any future attempt here collides with `tests/acceptance/crr/test_p1_121_art_121_3_unrated_inst_short_term.py` (VN counterparty, 85-day loan), which moves `0.2 → 1.0` — so **P1.334's regime gate must land in the same change**, or that pin has to be re-baselined to a value P1.334 says is wrong.
  - The attempt's diff is preserved outside the repo for reference; the fail-first test it came with (`test_p1_333_art_121_6_kleene_null_domesticity.py`) was removed with it, but its **discrimination design is worth rebuilding**: a 21-cell SCRA × sovereign-CQS grid where 9 cells move and 12 must not, so a fix that floors everything at the Art. 114(1) residual breaks 5 of them and a fix that *assigns* rather than *floors* breaks the whole Grade C row.
  - *Original bullet text follows for provenance:* **Art. 121(6) sovereign floor for institutions is silently DEAD for every non-EU counterparty (Kleene null) — ANTI-CONSERVATIVE under Basel 3.1** — `sa/risk_weights.py:959` builds `is_domestic_currency = is_uk_domestic | is_eu_domestic`; `engine/eu_sovereign.py:74-79` returns `country.replace_strict(_EU_COUNTRY_DOMESTIC_CURRENCY, default=None).eq(currency)`, so for a NON-EU country the mapped value is null, `null.eq("USD")` is null, and `False | null = null`. `_apply_sovereign_floor_for_institutions` (`:1496-1514`) then computes `_is_fx = ~is_domestic_currency` = null, `_floor_applies` is null, and `pl.when(null)` takes the `otherwise` branch. Instrumented: `cp_sovereign_cqs=6, currency=USD, cp_country_code=US` gives `is_domestic_currency -> None` and `risk_weight` stays 1.0 while `_sovereign_rw=1.5` — `max_horizontal` never applied. Dead for GB counterparties in a non-GBP currency and for every third-country institution. Under Basel 3.1 that is a live shortfall wherever SCRA Grade A (40%) or B (75%) sits below the jurisdiction's sovereign RW — exactly the population the article exists for. **Why the estate cannot see it**: all 20 sovereign-floor tests supply `local_currency` EXPLICITLY (`test_sovereign_floor_institutions.py`, `test_p1_254_*.py`), taking the deterministic `.is_not_null()` branch at `:1497` and stepping over the null path; `test_missing_columns_backward_compat` (`:382`) records a DIFFERENT asymmetry and was plausibly mistaken for this one. Fix `fill_null(False)` before the `|`, and add a third-country FX leg. **Effort: S** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 121(6) / BCBS CRE20.22

- **P1.375** [ ] **NEW (2026-09-09, fallout of P1.374) — Art. 501a(1)(b)-(o) and paragraphs 2-3 are unimplemented: the 0.75 is granted on limb (a) alone, so the factor is still available to exposures the article refuses. This is an INPUT-CONTRACT design item, not an engine change.** `grep -rn "501a" src/rwa_calc/ --include=*.py` returns only docstrings, two `EdgeColumn(citation=...)` labels (`contracts/edges.py:1164,2398`) and the flat multiplier. P1.374 enforced limb (1)(a) — corporate / specialised-lending class, exclusion of exposures in default (`engine/supporting_factors.py:474-521`). **Nothing tests limbs (b)-(o) or paragraph 2.** Read from `docs/assets/crr.pdf` pp. 417-420 (0-indexed) via pymupdf on the filing date, the residual is: **(b)** the obligor is an entity *created specifically* to finance or operate the infrastructure (the SPV limb); **(c)** ≥ two thirds of the repayment source is income from the financed assets, or subsidies/grants from a para-2(b)(i)/(ii) entity; **(d)** the obligor meets its obligations under severely stressed conditions; **(e)** cash flows are predictable and cover all future repayments, as *defined* by **paragraph 2** (availability-based / rate-of-return-regulated / take-or-pay / regulated-or-contractually-fixed-or-low-demand-risk output, **and** where revenues are not from a large number of users, an offtaker that is a 0%-RW sovereign or CQS ≥ 3, a ≤ 20%-RW PSE, an Art. 117(2) MDB, an Art. 118 international organisation, a CQS ≥ 3 corporate, or a replaceable counterparty); **(f)** refinancing risk low or mitigated; **(g)(i)-(vii)** seven contractual lender protections (termination protection, funded reserve accounts, control over assets and income, security, equity pledge, restricted use of net operating cash flow, restrictions on new debt); **(h)** the obligation is senior to all claims other than statutory and derivatives claims; **(i)(i)-(iii)** three construction-phase equity-investor tests; **(j)** completion safeguards; **(k)** operating-risk management; **(l)** tested technology; **(m)** permits obtained; **(n)** derivatives for risk mitigation only; **(o)** an environmental-objectives assessment across six named objectives. **The key design question, and the reason this is not an engine ticket:** almost all of these are *attestations* about the obligor and the facility that cannot be derived from anything in `LOAN_SCHEMA` / `FACILITY_SCHEMA` / `COUNTERPARTY_SCHEMA` — they need new input fields, so the first deliverable is a schema/contract proposal (nullable, conservative default, the P2.40a→P2.40b enablement-first pattern), not a predicate. **Verified against the actual carriers on `crm_exit` — the edge `apply_factors` reads — three limbs are the exceptions:** (h) **is derivable today** as a conservative one-way *denial* — `seniority` is a real input (`LOAN_SCHEMA:660`, `FACILITY_SCHEMA:513`, `CONTINGENTS_SCHEMA:795`, default `"senior"`, domain `VALID_SENIORITY = {"senior","subordinated"}`) and survives to `crm_exit`, and while `seniority == "senior"` is weaker than (h)'s full test, `seniority == "subordinated"` is unambiguously non-compliant, so denying on it is a strict, cheap improvement; (b) is **proxyable but not derivable** — `sl_type` and `cp_entity_type` are both on `crm_exit`, but `VALID_ENTITY_TYPES` has no special-purpose-vehicle member and `sl_type` exists only for SL rows, so a proxy would admit a *broader* population than the article and must not be used as a grant; (para 2(b)) the offtaker test is **computable by machinery the engine already owns** (0%/20% RW and CQS are all resolvable) but only once an offtaker counterparty reference exists as an input — it is a link field away, and is the highest-value single field on this list. Everything else — (c)-(g), (i)-(o) — is an attestation with no carrier. **Direction: every unimplemented limb is one the firm is not testing, so the 0.75 may still be granted where Art. 501a would refuse it — the residual of the same capital-understatement class P1.374 narrowed, and it is unbounded in the same direction.** Paragraph 3 (report the total Art. 501a exposure amount to competent authorities every six months) is also unimplemented; it is a reporting duty rather than a calculation, but record it here rather than discover it later. **Also in scope, because it is the downstream consumer that will otherwise re-seed the gap:** `.claude/skills/crr/references/supporting-factors.md:28-34` reduces Art. 501a to "The exposure must be flagged `is_infrastructure = true`", which is now materially wrong prose — it states no value so the generator is not involved, but the edit must be mirrored with `uv run python scripts/sync_agent_skills.py`. **Effort: L (input-contract design + one predicate per limb thereafter); S for the (h) subordination denial alone, which can land independently** | Ref: CRR Art. 501a(1)(a)-(o), Art. 501a(2)(a)-(b), Art. 501a(3) — `docs/assets/crr.pdf` pp. 417-420 · Ev: `src/rwa_calc/engine/supporting_factors.py:474-521`; `src/rwa_calc/contracts/edges.py:1164,2398`; `src/rwa_calc/data/schemas.py:473,619,886` (the three input schemas with no Art. 501a fields), `:513,660,795` (`seniority`), `:2140-2170` (`VALID_ENTITY_TYPES`, no SPV member); `.claude/skills/crr/references/supporting-factors.md:28-34`

The default band row 0170 ("100 (Default)") is emitted by NO run in either regime.** PS1/26 Art. 160(3) (`ps126app1.pdf` p.110): *"An institution shall use a PD of 100% for obligors in default"*; Art. 163(2) (p.115) states the retail twin. **Measured** on `reporting_irb_classes_portfolio.py` through the full orchestrator, both regimes: `IRC-LN-RRE-DEF` (`is_defaulted=True`, obligor `CP_RRE_DEF` `default_status=True`, A-IRB retail mortgage, `ead_final` 200,000, `expected_loss` 10,000 = BEEL 5% x EAD, `rwa_final` 0.0) carries `pd = pd_floored = 0.006` — the obligor's performing internal grade — so `reporting/corep/pd_scale.py` (which bands on `pd` under Basel 3.1 and `pd_floored` under CRR, never on `is_defaulted`) puts it in row **0060 "0.50 to <0.75"**: the `retail_mortgage` sheet emits row 0060 alone at col 0010 = 750,000 (all three RRE legs) and col 0050 exposure-weighted PD = 0.006, where Art. 160(3)/163(2) give row 0060 = 550,000 and row 0170 = 200,000. **Capital is not affected** (Art. 154(1)(a) K = max(0, LGD - BEEL) is computed off the default flag, and EL is BEEL) — this is a reporting-carrier defect. **Why it surfaced now:** BoE v4.1.1 corrected `boe_b0735` (`OF08.01.01.02 r0010 c0100 = sum(OF08.03 PD rows, c0020)`) to include r0170; with r0170 never emitted the rule went from VACUOUS to NOT_EVALUATED on its only run and left the vacuity register. `boe_b0739` (EL tie-out to OF 08.03 col 0100, also over r0170) has been never-evaluated for the same reason since v4.0.0. ### Tier 2 — Test Coverage Gaps (no code changes, but essential for confidence)

<!-- P5.40-P5.49 below are the GATE gaps found by the correctness-proposal batch
     (2026-08-09). Several were found by adversarial review of gates built in
     the same batch — i.e. the gate was analytically incapable of its job before

- **P5.40** [ ] **`PASS -> NOT_EVALUATED` escapes all six supervisory ratchet legs — the sibling of the vacuity hole just closed** — the 0.3 vacuity ratchet closes `PASS -> VACUOUS`; a rule that stops resolving ENTIRELY leaves the PASS population without entering the vacuity population, and the coverage ratchet only fires when a whole TEMPLATE goes unchecked. Measured: dropping ONE emitted row (C 02.00 r0160) from `crr/rich` moves two ERROR rules `PASS -> NOT_EVALUATED`, including `v3335_i` which passed on **rich only** so afterwards it has no verdict anywhere in the 16-run matrix — all six legs green, `templates_uncovered: ()` unmoved. Nulling C 02.00's only value column darkens 8 rules, also green. **Exposure: 221 rules (130 ERROR) hold a real verdict on exactly ONE run**, each one row-drop from going dark. Partial mitigation only: `coverage_report.py --check` ratchets `never_evaluated_rules` (max 785) and `union_binding_rules_*` (min 257/289) and by those definitions the row-drop moves both — but that is inference from the metric definitions, they are COUNTS so one rule going dark nets against another activating, and it is a different gate from the mandatory Tier 2 register. Fix: ratchet the per-rule `(regime, rule_id) -> has-a-verdict-anywhere` SET, not a count. **Effort: M** | Ref: `tests/acceptance/reporting/test_supervisory_validations.py` legs (f)/(g)
- **P5.42** [ ] **Input-domain census: a third form of dead branch that no cell or rule census can see** — distinct from the three forms in the LESSONS Graduation ledger (portfolio unregistered / registered-but-cell-dead / registered-but-wrong-config). Here the REGULATORY INPUT VALUE that activates a branch is absent from every fixture, so the branch is dead at the input-domain level while the cells are populated and the rules pass. Worked example: Art. 121(1) Table 5 was exercised NOWHERE outside `tests/oracle/` — all 16 registered runs carry unrated institutions with a null `cp_sovereign_cqs`, and a scan of all 494 `tests/fixtures/**/*.parquet` found exactly ONE candidate whose loan is ~85 days so Art. 121(3) claims it first. Three wrong risk weights shipped through a ~9,600-test estate structurally unable to see them. Proposed gate: enumerate each pack `LookupTable` / `BandedTable` key domain and assert every key value is realised by at least one fixture row REACHING the branch that reads it; ratchet the unrealised set. This is the most generalisable finding of the batch — it explains how P1.316 survived. **Effort: M** | Ref: `rwa_calc/rulebook/packs/`, `scripts/coverage_report.py` as the model for the ratchet

- **P5.44** [ ] **`output_digest` is not reproducible, so the reachability probe cannot return UNREACHABLE at all** — blocks the whole defect-injection campaign (proposal item 0.1). Three clean computations of the wide digest gave three different values; float summation-order dust in 5 of 5,453 columns (`c08_01/corporate/0310` = 32528268.383276727 vs ...723; `c09_01/TOTAL/0090` = 19580833.333333336 vs ...332; `c09_02/GB/0125` = 43902551.59373399 vs ...996). `scripts/defect_injection.py:366-371` hashes `str(frame.get_column(column).to_list())` — exact equality on `repr(float)` — and the bias is ONE-DIRECTIONAL toward REACHABLE, which collapses the three-verdict design: truly-dead mutants enter the scored denominator, cannot be caught by anything, and surface as PHANTOM ESCAPES. Decisive independent proof: `generic-correlation-decay` reports REACHABLE although `1.0-exp(-50)` and `1.0-exp(-45)` are both exactly 1.0 in float64. **Verified fix**: round floats (6dp) before hashing — the digest becomes reproducible and BOTH controls then behave as the catalogue requires. **Do NOT flip `expect_unreachable` on `control-unreachable-output-floor-full`**: once the digest is fixed the mutant is correctly UNREACHABLE, and flipping it would score a mutant that `tests/unit/rulebook/test_output_floor_features.py:59` catches trivially, inflating the rate. Also fix `generic-retail-correlation-decay`'s `expect_unreachable=True`, which is wrong on its own terms (`1.0-exp(-35)` vs `1.0-exp(-30)` differ by ~1e-13). **Effort: S** | Ref: `scripts/defect_injection.py::output_digest`, `scripts/defect_catalogue.py:361-379`
- **P5.45** [ ] **The injection harness's restore is not byte-exact and its "byte verification" cannot see the one corruption it causes** — `injected()` does `original = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")`, whose universal-newline translation turns CRLF into LF in memory, then writes `original` back on restore — so the restore is **structurally incapable** of putting CRLF back for a CRLF file. And the post-restore check compares `read_text()` against that same already-normalised copy, i.e. a TEXT comparison blind to the corruption; it passed while `engine/irb/formulas.py` differed from HEAD by 1,323 bytes. The docstring's "post-restore verification that the bytes really came back" overstates the code. Blast radius: 2 of 10 mutation targets are CRLF in HEAD (`engine/irb/formulas.py`, `engine/sa/crr_risk_weight_tables.py`), reachable by 8 of 22 mutants. Fix: read and write bytes, and compare bytes. **Effort: S** | Ref: `scripts/defect_injection.py::injected`

- **P5.51** [ ] **Two CRITICAL `python:S3776` cognitive-complexity findings in `scripts/injection_ratchet.py` — deliberately deferred, and the reasoning matters more than the fix.** SonarCloud reports complexity 36 (`check`, ~line 406) and 38 (~line 556) against an allowed 15. NOT gate-blocking: the quality gate passes and `new_maintainability_rating` is 1 (A). Left alone because **the branch count IS the specification, not accidental complexity** — `check()` has EIGHT distinct failure paths, each individually verified to exit non-zero (detected-set loss, escaped-set gain, unreachable-set gain, a gate TIMEOUT, a partial scorecard, the unreachable control coming back reachable, a known-defect escape below the floor, and a malformed `banked` status). Splitting them to satisfy a threshold risks silently dropping one, which converts a loud gate into a quiet one — the exact failure class this workstream exists to eliminate. **If taken on, do not extract by line count**: extract one NAMED predicate per failure path (`_detected_set_shrank`, `_control_became_reachable`, …), each returning a finding or `None`, held in a single explicit registry so the eight remain enumerable in one place — then assert the registry's SIZE in a test, so losing a check is a red rather than an omission. Re-verify all eight go red individually afterwards, as the original build did. Acceptable alternative: accept both in the SonarCloud UI with this reasoning, the same disposition the project already uses for its taint findings. **Effort: M (or S to accept)** | Ref: `scripts/injection_ratchet.py`; SonarCloud PR #465

#### Tier 2 — coverage and harness gaps raised by batch `facility-share-fanout-20260905`

- **P5.62** [ ] **NEW (2026-09-05, owed coverage from the facility-share fan-out; design doc `docs/plans/facility-share-riskiest-member.md` §10.3) — no fixture can reach the one path on which the fan-out LOWERS RWA: a revolving retail member demoted out of QRRE at high PD.** Under decision D4 a candidate row counts toward its own member's obligor aggregates with no special-casing. The QRRE aggregate (`engine/classify/subtypes.py::qrre_obligor_aggregate_limit_expr`, `:325-408`) dedupes on `["counterparty_reference", "parent_facility_reference"]` (`:391-400`) before summing over the obligor (`:407`); a candidate undrawn row carries the source facility as its `parent_facility_reference` (`engine/hierarchy/facility_undrawn.py:506-507`) and the facility's limit as `facility_limit` (`:629`). **So the candidate adds a contribution only where its member has no other QRRE-candidate row under that same parent facility** — the clear case being the facility owner made a member by D3, and the general case being a member whose own leg fails `is_qrre_candidate` while the undrawn row passes. Where it does add one, `is_qrre` can flip false (`subtypes.py:210`) and the row falls back to `retail_other`, whose IRB correlation is `0.03·f + 0.16·(1−f)` on the decay-35 term (`engine/irb/formulas.py:733`, `:739`) against QRRE's fixed value (`formulas.py:779-780`). The two cross at PD ≈ 7.3%; **above the crossover, demotion LOWERS RWA.** FS-1 cannot carry a retail member — adding one breaks the `u_IRB < u_SA < x·s_IRB` divergence chain the floor-aware test needs — so the path ships unmeasured. **Owed:** a fixture with a shared facility whose members include a revolving unsecured natural-person obligor near the QRRE limit at a PD above the crossover, plus an acceptance test asserting the RWA direction, carrying an adequacy assertion that the member actually flips (a fixture where `is_qrre` is False in both states proves nothing — LESSONS C11). ⚠ **Build it on P5.54's portfolio if that lands first** — P5.54 already owes a headroom-bearing QRRE reporting portfolio, and two QRRE fixtures with different limits invite the wrong one being read. Read the limit from `regulatory_threshold(pack, "qrre_max_limit", …)`, never typed (LESSONS A4). This item buys the **measurement**; if it shows a material reduction the remedy is a new Tier 1 bullet. **Effort: S** | Ref: CRR Art. 154(4); PS1/26 Art. 147(5A) · Ev: `src/rwa_calc/engine/classify/subtypes.py:210`, `:325-408`; `src/rwa_calc/engine/irb/formulas.py:733`, `:739`, `:779-780`; `src/rwa_calc/engine/hierarchy/facility_undrawn.py:506-507`, `:629`
- **P5.63** [ ] **NEW (2026-09-05, retro graduation candidate from the test wave) — `tests/oracle/derive.py` regeneration is machine-dependent at ULP scale, so the next agent to run it re-introduces a diff it cannot explain.** Measured this batch: a purely **additive** change to `tests/oracle/derivations/irb.py` produced a regeneration that moved **12 untouched records** by ~1e-16 relative — last-bit differences in `statistics.NormalDist` on this machine, nothing to do with the edit. The wave restored them by hand, which is precisely the failure mode that does not survive being a convention: the next agent sees a 12-record diff, cannot tell it from a real derivation change, and either banks it or spends the batch on it. Three candidate graduations, cheapest first: **(a)** have `derive.py` splice back only records whose derivation **source** changed, leaving untouched records byte-identical (structural, no environment pinning, and it is the one that keeps working on a different machine); **(b)** make the doc-hash test tolerate ULP-only drift on records whose derivation source is unchanged; **(c)** pin the regeneration environment. Prefer (a) — (b) weakens a gate and (c) is a convention with no enforcement. ⚠ Agents are barred from editing `tests/oracle/` by the do-not-do register in `docs/plans/target-architecture-migration.md`, so this is operator or explicitly-scoped work. **Effort: S** | Ev: `tests/oracle/derive.py`; `tests/oracle/derivations/irb.py`; `.claude/state/facility-share-fanout-20260905.json` (`notes`, "RETRO CANDIDATE … derive.py")

Neither `scripts/check_template_cell_coverage.py` nor `scripts/coverage_report.py` can see it: a cell is live in both states and a rule holds a verdict in both. **Proposed gate:** a census over `OutputFloorSummary.portfolio_floor_binding` (`contracts/bundles.py:886`) across `RUNS`, asserting at least one binding **and** at least one non-binding Basel 3.1 run, ratcheted as a set rather than a count (LESSONS B8). Consider generalising to a small named-state census — the same shape would cover P5.42's input-domain gap and P5.40's has-a-verdict-anywhere set. **Effort: S** | Ev: `src/rwa_calc/contracts/bundles.py:886`; `tests/acceptance/reporting/test_supervisory_validations.py` (`RUNS`); `.claude/state/fs1-gate-w3/test-writer-report-3.md` · **2026-11-16:** `tests/acceptance/reporting/test_of08_binding_floor.py` forces the floor to bind on `irb-shapes` (`sa_t2_credit` 1.5m), `rich` (6m) and `ccr` (400k), test-local only. - **P5.69** [ ] **NEW (2026-09-09, fallout of P1.374) — a test harness can feed `exposure_class` a value production cannot produce, take the wrong branch of an exact-match gate, and still pass. Two instances in the P1.374 branch alone, which is what makes it a pattern rather than a slip.** `exposure_class` is **engine-derived only** — it is absent from `LOAN_SCHEMA`, `FACILITY_SCHEMA` and `COUNTERPARTY_SCHEMA` (the only two input schemas carrying the name are `CIU_HOLDINGS_SCHEMA:1385` and `MODEL_PERMISSIONS_SCHEMA:1472`, neither an exposure) and is written by the classifier as `ExposureClass.<X>.value`, i.e. always lowercase and always non-null. So every exact-`.value` gate reading it is safe on a real run — `engine/classify/approach.py:303,313,319`, `engine/re_split/flagging.py:199-209`, `engine/aggregator/aggregator.py:715,726`. **The entire risk is in the harness**, where the column is hand-written. The two instances: `tests/fixtures/single_exposure.py::calculate_single_slotting_exposure` omitted `exposure_class` altogether, so every caller silently took the permissive column-absent fallback of P1.374's new gate — while the builder's own docstring claimed production-realistic contract columns; and `pad_slotting_branch_defaults` (`tests/fixtures/contract_columns.py:119-143`) padded it to a typed **null**, failing three slotting tests on a state production never produces (`approach.py:319` routes to slotting on the exact value `specialised_lending`). Both are fixed on that branch; the *class* of defect is not. **Re-measured independently for this filing** (tight pattern — a string literal directly bound to or compared against `exposure_class`, over all of `tests/**/*.py`): **325 UPPERCASE occurrences across 14 distinct values against 591 lowercase across 21**, and mixed-case is nil (the only two hits are the unrelated column name `Asset_Class`). Of the 14 uppercase values, **11 are case-variants of real `ExposureClass` members** — those are the dangerous ones, since they read as valid and are one exact-match gate away from taking a branch production never takes. **The other three are legitimate and must not be "fixed": `RETAIL`, `COMMERCIAL_RE`, `RESIDENTIAL_RE` are keys of the SA risk-weight *table*, a separate namespace** (`engine/sa/crr_risk_weight_tables.py:436,518`) that the join case-folds into at `engine/sa/risk_weights.py:1044` — so a test asserting an uppercase value is sometimes correctly querying that table. 18 assertions read the column back and compare uppercase; 4 legitimately query the table, 14 sit in `tests/unit/test_real_estate_splitter.py`, where one adjacent line pair captures the whole confusion: `assert secured["exposure_class"] == "residential_mortgage"` (engine-written) beside `assert residual["exposure_class"] == "CORPORATE"` (a pass-through of the fixture's own input). **Graduation-shaped fix:** a contract test asserting every `exposure_class` literal under `tests/` is either an `ExposureClass` value or a member of a declared, short allowlist of SA risk-weight-table keys — an executable check, not prose, because the estate is 916 literals wide and no reviewer will hold the two namespaces apart by eye. Not written here: it is a 275+-site sweep and it will fail on first run, so it needs its own item. **One hypothesis was chased and REFUTED — do not re-file it:** `re_split/flagging.py`'s case-sensitive exclusion list looked escapable by an uppercase class, but all six spellings split identically, because the gate runs *inside* the classifier (`classifier.py:74`) on the class the classifier has just written — it never sees a harness-supplied value. **Effort: M** | Ev: `tests/fixtures/single_exposure.py`, `tests/fixtures/contract_columns.py:119-143`; `src/rwa_calc/engine/classify/approach.py:303,313,319`; `src/rwa_calc/engine/sa/risk_weights.py:1044`; `src/rwa_calc/engine/sa/crr_risk_weight_tables.py:436,518`; `tests/unit/test_real_estate_splitter.py`
- **P5.71** [ ] **NEW (2026-09-09, P1.373 delivery) — `cells_live` ratchets NULL-ness, not ZERO-ness, so the coverage ratchet is structurally blind to a column that publishes `0.00` for its entire life. The two cell ratchets are measurably NOT redundant.** `scripts/coverage_report.py::_absorb_frame` (`:338-360` — the function is `_absorb_frame`; there is no `_fold_sheet`) marks a cell live on `value is not None` (`:356-358`) and separately records `cells_nonzero` on `value != 0` (`:359-360`). **`cells_nonzero` is computed and is not ratcheted:** `_RATCHET_MIN` is `union_binding_rules_crr` / `union_binding_rules_b31` / `cells_live` / `template_cell_liveness_bp` (`:759-763`) and `_RATCHET_MAX` is `dead_cells` / `never_evaluated_rules` (`:765`); the non-zero count surfaces only as the advisory `cells["live_but_always_zero"]` evidence field (`:471-474`), whose own comment already says *“a cell that is only ever 0.00 is present but asserts nothing about the portfolio”*. **Measured on the P1.373 branch:** two fixture legs that light five previously-dark supporting-factor columns moved `cells_live` by **21 cells, every one of them `crr/C 07.00.a[corporate][r0035][*]`** — and by **zero** for the IRB leg, because C 08.01 cols 0256/0257 had been emitting a structural `0.00` for the template's entire life and therefore already counted as live. The stricter `scripts/check_template_cell_coverage.py` (unit = the (template, column) pair, on a NON-ZERO test) caught all five: `c07_00/0217`, `c08_01/0257`, `c08_02/0257`, `c09_01/0082`, `c09_02/0122`. So on this one change **each gate saw something the other was blind to** — record that before anyone proposes retiring either as duplicative. **Candidate fix:** add `cells_nonzero` to `_RATCHET_MIN`. It is an absolute count of the same shape as `cells_live`, so the module's own warning that `template_cell_liveness_bp` and `dead_cells` are sometimes ANTI-signal (`:729-752`) does not apply to it; re-measure the baseline with `--update-baseline` rather than typing the number, and note the metric is a UNION over the portfolio×regime matrix so it moves with `RUNS` like its siblings. If the answer is no, the reason belongs in `BASELINE_COMMENT` beside the existing READ-THIS-BEFORE-TRUSTING-A-GREEN note. **This is LESSONS B5's still-open case (i), the cell-granular one** (`.claude/LESSONS.md:874`), which until now had no file and line number. **Effort: S** | Ev: `scripts/coverage_report.py:242-244`, `:338-360`, `:471-474`, `:729-765`; `scripts/check_template_cell_coverage.py`; `.claude/LESSONS.md:874`

- **P5.70** [ ] **NEW (2026-09-09, graduation candidate from P1.372) — a `(ref, name)` tuple whose ref reads as a row address and is consumed by nobody: the same trap twice in one file, and no gate in the estate could see either instance.** Both retired maps (`SA_EXPOSURE_CLASS_ROWS` and `IRB_EXPOSURE_CLASS_ROWS`, `reporting/corep/templates.py:173-189` pre-fix) declared row refs that **no template's row list contains**, and in several cases refs that address a *different* row in a real template — `equity`→`0110`, which is `high_risk` on `CRR_C09_01_ROWS`; `corporate`→`0030`, which is "Off balance sheet items subject to credit risk" on C 08.01. Nothing caught it because **nothing read the ref half at all**, and a value that is never consumed cannot be wrong at runtime — only a structural check can reach it. **Graduation target: a contract test asserting that every declared row ref in a class→row map resolves to a row that exists in some live template's row list**, with the corollary limb that a map on the SHEET (z) axis must carry **no ref at all**. ⚠ **Close kin to the outstanding LESSONS B2 candidate** (assert every class/row map's key set against `ExposureClass`, never against a hand-written list). **File and build them as ONE check with two limbs — keys are real classes, refs are real rows.** The expensive half is shared and is neither assertion: it is enumerating the class-keyed maps (`templates.py`, `sheet_labels.py`, the `c08_07`/`c02` row lists) and tagging each with the axis it addresses. Splitting the item makes two agents write that walker twice, and P1.372's evidence is that both failures co-occurred in the **same declaration**. The limbs must stay distinct assertions, though, because they run in opposite directions: keys are **total** over the enum on a sheet axis (`C07_00_SA_SHEET_MAP`) but only **contained** where a regime legitimately omits a class (`IRB_EXPOSURE_CLASS_LABELS` names no B31 IRB sovereign sheet, correctly — PS1/26 withdraws it). **Why this must be executable and not prose:** a test-writer on the P1.372 branch found that **nothing in the repo asserted `C07_00_SA_SHEET_MAP` is total over `ExposureClass`** — the sibling contract test `tests/contracts/test_c07_art112_sheet_axis.py:176-177` asserts keys ⊆ enum (the "invented" direction) and never the superset — and that `get_c07_sheet_labels`, added by PR #497, **had no test at all** (zero hits under `tests/` at that commit). That branch covers both, but only for those two constants. The generalisable finding is the pattern: **map-shaped constants get their *contents* reviewed and their *contract* ungated.** **Effort: M** | Ref: `.claude/LESSONS.md` B2 (an unmatched dict key zero-fills) and B3 (a test that shares production's assumption validates nothing) · Ev: `src/rwa_calc/reporting/corep/templates.py:175-195` (the post-fix comment recording both defects), `:1267-1283`, `:1807`; `tests/contracts/test_c07_art112_sheet_axis.py:166-177`; `src/rwa_calc/reporting/corep/sheet_labels.py`

#### Tier 2 — harness gaps raised by the 2026-11-13 batch start-method postmortem

- **P5.72** [ ] **NEW (2026-11-13, `/postmortem` — the gate that should have caught the batch hang) — CI never runs the interpreter `requires-python` declares as the floor, and the interpreter it does run is chosen by the runner image.** `pyproject.toml` declares `requires-python = ">=3.13"`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` names no interpreter and there is no `.python-version`, so `uv sync` takes the runner's system `python3`. Evidence, `master` run 43438755694, identical in all eight jobs: `Using CPython 3.14.4 interpreter at: /usr/bin/python3`. So (a) nothing gates 3.13 at all — the `run_batch` hang lived there from 0.3.29 to 0.3.34 while two tests that detect it ran green on every push — and (b) CI's interpreter moves whenever the image moves, silently, in either direction. **Decision needed (CI cost), which is why this is filed and not done:** add a floor-interpreter leg — as a NEW job (e.g. `Tests (Python floor)`, `uv sync --extra dev --python 3.13`), **not** a matrix on `Tests`, which would rename that check and break any branch-protection rule naming it — at roughly one more ~6-minute Tests job per push; and/or pin the primary interpreter (a `.python-version`, or `python-version:` on `setup-uv`) so it moves by commit. Whether to run the full suite or a marked subset on the floor is part of the decision. **Known-green starting point:** the dev-loop suite passes on Linux / CPython 3.13.18 as of this change-set (the environment the hang was found on). **Not needed for the batch defect itself** — `WORKER_START_METHOD` removed the interpreter dependence — this is for the next 3.13-versus-3.14 difference. **Effort: S.** Ref: `docs/development/escape-log.md` 2026-11-13; `.claude/LESSONS.md` Graduation ledger 2026-11-13.
- **P5.73** [ ] **NEW (2026-11-13, `/postmortem` — separate hazard found while digging; filed, not fixed) — a batch worker that DEADLOCKS, as opposed to one that dies, has no bound, no error code and no test, so a hang stalls instead of failing.** `api/batch.py::_run_parallel` waits in `as_completed(futures)` with no timeout and `_recover_lost_scopes` in `.result()` likewise. A worker that exits hard is contained (`BrokenProcessPool` → recovery → `BATCH005`); a worker that stops making progress is not, and `run_batch` then never returns. The start-method hang was one instance; `engine/materialise.py` documents Polars SIGSEGV on deep plans, and a plan that spins is the same pathology without the exit. **Two halves, separable.** (i) Product: an optional per-scope or per-batch deadline on `BatchRequest`, surfaced as a new `BATCH00x` code per scope, with the stuck worker terminated and the others' results kept — needs a decision on the default (none, to preserve today's behaviour, or a generous ceiling). (ii) Harness: the real-pool tests in `tests/unit/api/test_batch.py` bound nothing and the suite has no timeout plugin, so under xdist `--dist=loadfile` a hang presents as the run stalling at ~99% — read as a slow box, not a red test. A bounded wait in those tests (or `pytest-timeout` on the file) turns the next hang into a failure with a name. **Acceptance for (ii):** revert `WORKER_START_METHOD` on Linux / Python 3.13 and the equivalence test must FAIL inside its bound rather than hang. **Effort: S (ii) / M (i).** Ref: `docs/development/escape-log.md` 2026-11-13.
- **P5.74** [ ] **NEW (2026-11-13, surfaced by review of the Art. 219 netting-currency fixes) — `tests/contracts/test_pipeline_collect_budget.py` is ORDER-DEPENDENT: run after a particular sequence of other test files in one process, it reads roughly double the banked materialisation counts and fails both regimes.** Measured serially (`-n 0 -p no:randomly`) on a 67-file sequence of unit/integration/contracts files ending in the budget test — the sequence one xdist worker was given in a review run: observed `collect 234 / collect_schema 226 / collect_all 19` against banked `83 / 113 / 9` (B31). **Pre-existing:** the identical replay fails the same way on master `099b0d4`. It does NOT reproduce under `-n auto` / `-n 4` on a 4-core box in two runs, so it surfaces only when xdist scheduling happens to co-locate the leaking files — i.e. a latent CI flake that any new test file can expose. Likely some earlier test leaves process-global state switched on that adds materialisations to every later pipeline run (an audit sink, a materialisation recorder, a cache) — find it by bisecting the sequence, then either reset that state in the budget fixture's Arrange step or fix the leaking test's teardown. Make the budget test assert its own precondition (the global is off) so a future leak fails with a named cause instead of a count. **Effort: S** | Ev: `tests/contracts/test_pipeline_collect_budget.py::measurement`
- **P5.75** [ ] **NEW (2026-11-14, nightly-repair intake) — two C 02.00 IRB rows are empty in every RUNS portfolio, so no gate can see a defect in them.** This is the B5 "registered, dead cell" form. Measured with the deterministic probe, `defect_injection.py --reachability-only`, over the whole RUNS matrix:
  - `known-c02-phantom-class-key` (C 02.00 r0330 A-IRB institutions, keyed on a class string no enum member produces) moves nothing.
  - `generic-firb-corporate-stranded` (r0290 F-IRB specialised lending stranded at zero) moves nothing.

And no portfolio carries a non-slotting IRB specialised-lending leg. The known defect the first mutant twins (A-IRB central governments keyed on `'central_government'`, fixed 2026-11-13) escaped for the template's whole life for this reason.

Give a registered reporting portfolio a CRR A-IRB institution exposure and an F-IRB (non-slotting) specialised-lending exposure, then re-bank the coverage ratchet.

  **Acceptance:**
  - `--reachability-only --mutants known-c02-phantom-class-key generic-firb-corporate-stranded` reports both REACHABLE.
  - Each, applied by hand, turns `tests/acceptance/reporting/test_supervisory_validations.py` red.

  **Effort: S–M** | Ref: `scripts/defect_catalogue.py`; `src/rwa_calc/reporting/corep/c02_rows.py` (`AIRB_INSTITUTIONS`, `FIRB_SPECIALISED_LENDING`); `.claude/LESSONS.md` B5, B6
- **P5.76** [ ] **NEW (2026-11-14, nightly-repair intake) — six more defect-injection mutants move nothing in the RUNS matrix. Each needs re-targeting or fixture coverage, or the injection nightly stays red on it.** Measured with the deterministic probe:

But any that NO gate catches scores UNREACHABLE and fails `injection_ratchet.py --check` ("expected to be reachable moved nothing"). Per mutant, either re-target it at a site the pipeline runs, or give a RUNS portfolio the input that exercises it.

#### Tier 2 — 2026-08-08 independent validation system harness gaps (PR #457, C1–C6)

> The validation harnesses landed but four capability gaps were left as deliberate follow-on commits (recorded in `docs/plans/independent-validation-system.md` and the PR). These are tooling/coverage items, not calculation defects — but until they run, the estate's ability to catch a wrong number is unmeasured. C6 in particular is the component that tells us whether any of the others work (plan §7: "if only one thing is built, build C6").

- **P5.18** [ ] **NEW (C6)** — The defect-injection scorecard exists (`scripts/defect_injection.py` + `scripts/defect_catalogue.py`) but has **never been run as a campaign**: no detection rate is measured and no nightly gate exists. Wire a nightly CI job running both `--ladder legacy` and `--ladder full` (a before/after detection rate from one build), publish the detection rate, the mean detection tier and — the load-bearing output — the list of ESCAPED mutants (changed output, every gate stayed green). Honour the three-verdict design (DETECTED / ESCAPED / UNREACHABLE) and the reachability probe so a vacuous mutant is not scored as an escape. **Effort: M** | Ref: `docs/plans/independent-validation-system.md` §C6, §7; `scripts/defect_injection.py` — **Status 2026-11-14:** the campaign is wired and runs nightly (`.github/workflows/nightly-injection.yml`, ratchet `scripts/injection_ratchet.py`). The baseline is still `UNBANKED`, by design. PR #526 fixed three harness defects that made every scorecard unreadable:
  - a non-deterministic reachability digest, so every mutant probed REACHABLE;
  - a CRLF target restored as LF, which left `engine/irb/formulas.py` dirty;

### Tier 3 — COREP Reporting Completeness

- **P2.56** [ ] ⚠⚠ **HEAD OF TIER 3 — LAND THIS NEXT, BEFORE OR ALONGSIDE THE MERGE OF PR #501.** **NEW (2026-09-09, P1.373 delivery; re-scoped the same day from “theoretical” to CONSTRUCTED AND MEASURED) — the COREP supporting-factor attribution keys col 0216 on the RAW `is_infrastructure` flag as a PROXY for “the relief came from Art. 501a”. Once #501's Art. 501a(1)(a) class gate is in the tree the engine and the reporting layer disagree, and an ordinary CRR row reports **Art. 501 SME relief in col 0217, the Art. 501a column**.** **OPERATOR DECISION (2026-09-09): file, do NOT fix in the P1.373 PR.** Four reasons, recorded so the deferral is not re-litigated: (a) blast radius is **attribution only** — the footing identity `0215+0216+0217 = 0220` holds exactly, no total moves, **no capital effect**, and all six EBA footing rules stay PASS; it misstates the 0216/0217 SPLIT for one constructible population. (b) **No row in the estate is affected today**, and none after #501 merges either, until somebody adds one. (c) The correct fix is **engine-side** and touches `engine/supporting_factors.py` — the same capital-critical file #501 changes on another unmerged branch — so doing it in the reporting PR buys a merge conflict on the worst possible file for no gain. (d) **P1.373 as it stands is a strict improvement**: six real rule breaks to zero. **The cost of the deferral is a knowably-wrong attribution for a constructible row from the moment #501 merges** — which is why this bullet sits at the head of its tier rather than in the queue. **MEASURED on the branch that contains the gate** (`worktree-p1373-supporting-factor-attribution`, its own venv, real `PipelineOrchestrator().run_with_data`). **EXACT REPRO — minutes, not a re-derivation:** config `CalculationConfig.crr(reporting_date=date(2025,12,31), permission_mode=PermissionMode.STANDARDISED)`; take the reporting portfolio's `RP-LN-SME` (corporate, `annual_revenue` 30,000,000, `drawn_amount` 500,000) and give it (1) `product_type='INFRASTRUCTURE_LOAN'`, (2) `property_type='residential'` + `ltv=0.60`, and (3) **a linked real-estate collateral row** — `collateral_type='real_estate'`, `property_type='residential'`, `market_value=833_333.0`, `property_ltv=0.60`, `beneficiary_type='loan'`, `beneficiary_reference=LN_SME` — then `make_raw_bundle(...)` and run. **Result:** the exposure splits in two; the SECURED leg returns `exposure_class=residential_mortgage`, `is_sme=true`, `is_infrastructure=true`, performing, **`supporting_factor=0.7619`** — the Art. 501 SME tier-1 factor, NOT the 0.75 infrastructure factor, which is the shipped class gate denying Art. 501a and Art. 501 binding in its place — with `rwa_pre_factor` 175,000 → `rwa_final` 133,332.5, relief **41,667.5**; the post-P1.373 reporting predicates then book it `0217=True / 0216=False`. So 41,667.5 of **SME** relief is reported in the **infrastructure** column and excluded from the SME one. The RESIDUAL leg is the built-in control: `corporate_sme`, gate passes, `supporting_factor=0.75`, correctly 0217, relief 0. **Mechanism — the RE loan-splitter, ordinary CRR behaviour, not a corner.** `engine/re_split/splitter.py` rewrites `exposure_class` to `_SECURED_TARGET_RESIDENTIAL` / `_SECURED_TARGET_COMMERCIAL` (`:171`, `:179` → `residential_mortgage` / `commercial_mortgage`) on secured rows (`:691`) and on the B31 whole-loan path (`:325`) — both OUTSIDE #501's three-class set. It runs `crm_processor -> re_splitter -> calculators` (`engine/stages/re_split.py` docstring): **after** classify, **before** every `apply_factors` call site (`engine/sa/factors_output.py:83`, `engine/irb/calculator.py:171`, `engine/slotting/calculator.py:177`). `is_sme` has **exactly one writer in the whole engine**, `engine/classify/subtypes.py:263`, so it survives the rewrite untouched. `is_candidate` (`engine/re_split/flagging.py:198-219`) excludes only the already-RE classes plus defaulted / equity / covered-bond / high-risk / income-producing / ADC, so **`corporate_sme` is a split candidate**, and `is_npsme` (`:220-226`) reads `is_sme` outright. Under CRR the mode is `split` whenever any component is eligible (`:322-329`) and the RRE limb carries no extra test (`rre_eligible = is_candidate & has_rre`, `:256`); only CRE carries the Art. 126(2)(d) rental-coverage requirement (`:257-262`). **THREE REASONS THE EARLIER PROBE MISSED IT — keep all three; the first is a reusable gate shape, not a one-off slip.** **(1) A PROBE THAT CANNOT FAIL.** Run in a tree WITHOUT #501, the escape search is **vacuous by construction**: pre-gate, any `is_infrastructure` row takes the flat 0.75, which always wins `min_horizontal` against an SME blend bounded below by 0.7619, so **every** shape returns non-divergent regardless of class — the probe's green says nothing about its subject. Measured, not argued: the identical construction in `worktree-p1373-sf-attribution-reporting` returns `supporting_factor=0.75` on both legs, correctly attributed. A search for an escape must be run against the code that could produce it, and must be shown capable of a red. **(2)** Property on the LOAN row alone never splits — a linked `beneficiary_type='loan'` collateral row is required, as `tests/fixtures/reporting_portfolio.py::_collateral` says outright. **(3)** The estate's only SME re-split row, `RESP-LN-CRE-SME`, is CRE and does **not** split under CRR (measured `re_split_role = null`, class stays `corporate_sme`) because of the Art. 126(2)(d) rental-coverage test. Residential is the unguarded path; commercial is the guarded one, and the estate only covers the guarded one. **PRESCRIBED FIX — engine-side, and it is the engine half P1.373 prescribed but did not deliver.** Have `apply_factors` emit the real `sme_supporting_factor_applied` / `infrastructure_factor_applied` flags — which arm of `min_horizontal` bound, ties broken deterministically to ONE column — instead of only the generic `supporting_factor_applied` (`engine/supporting_factors.py:438-445`). **The reporting layer then needs NO change**: `sf_adjustment_terms`' first limb already prefers those names when present and simply becomes the live one, and the `exclude_infra` proxy is deleted rather than patched. Three consequences to bank in the same pass: it **retires P6.55** by making the `dedicated` limb live instead of dead; it means the **P5.66 unit builders** (`tests/unit/reporting/corep/_builders.py::_sa_results_with_phase2_cols`, which already set the dedicated columns) start exercising the REAL production path instead of a dead limb — today the entire COREP unit surface for these columns tests code production never runs, which is P1.373's own “why no gate saw it” finding; and it removes the engine↔reporting disagreement at the source rather than keeping two definitions of “which factor applied” in sync by hand. Not a one-liner: the names exist aspirationally in `data/schemas.py::CRR_OUTPUT_SCHEMA_ADDITIONS` (`:3702-3713`) but on **no** edge — `contracts/edges.py` declares `is_sme` / `is_infrastructure` / `rwa_pre_factor` / `supporting_factor_applied` (`:1153`, `:1164`, `:1706`, `:1726`, `:2397-2404`) and not the dedicated pair, so the seal would strip them before reporting (same shape as P2.54's `ciu_approach`); `analysis/legacy_ledger.py:542-555` encodes the same either/or and moves with it. **Not ruled out, not explored:** the second post-classify writer of `exposure_class` is IRB guarantee substitution (`engine/irb/guarantee.py:710`, restored at `:749`) — if the substituted class survives to `apply_factors`, a guaranteed SME infrastructure loan is a second instance of the same shape. ✅ **P1.211's class list is NOT the problem — an earlier draft of this bullet said it was, and that was wrong.** #501 ships `{CORPORATE, CORPORATE_SME, SPECIALISED_LENDING}` case-folded via `.str.to_uppercase().is_in(...)`, so `corporate_sme` — where every SME row lands — IS admitted and the estate's `LN_SME_INFRA` leg keeps its Art. 501a relief (verified on the branch). The defect is not the class list; it is that the REPORTING layer keys on the raw flag while the ENGINE now keys on flag AND class AND default. **Tier 3 not Tier 1** on P2.54's precedent (RWA-neutral → Tier 3), but at the HEAD of Tier 3 because it goes live on a merge rather than on a code change. **Effort: M** | Ref: CRR Art. 501, Art. 501a(1)(a); COREP Annex II C 07.00 cols 0216/0217, C 08.01/02 cols 0256/0257, C 09.01 cols 0081/0082, C 09.02 cols 0121/0122 · Ev: `src/rwa_calc/reporting/corep/supporting_factors.py:99-124`; `src/rwa_calc/engine/supporting_factors.py:419`, `:432-445`; `src/rwa_calc/engine/re_split/splitter.py:171`, `:179`, `:325`, `:691`; `src/rwa_calc/engine/re_split/flagging.py:198-226`, `:256-262`, `:322-329`; `src/rwa_calc/engine/classify/subtypes.py:263`; `src/rwa_calc/engine/stages/re_split.py`; `src/rwa_calc/contracts/edges.py:2397-2404`; `src/rwa_calc/data/schemas.py:3702-3713`; `tests/fixtures/reporting_portfolio.py`

The PS1/26 twin is independently evidenced: **Art. 147(2)(ea)** creates a dedicated IRB CIU class, **Art. 147(6)(a)** expressly carves CIUs out of equity, and **Art. 147A(i)** gives (ea) *"the approach set out in Article 152"* while (h) gives equity the Standardised Approach — i.e. **Art. 147A removed the IRB route for equity and KEPT it for CIU**. Corroborated by the disclosure instructions: OV1 row 12 cites *"Article 132A(1) of the Credit Risk: Standardised Approach (CRR) Part **or Article 152(2) of the Credit Risk: Internal Ratings Based Approach (CRR) Part**"*. `entity_type_to_irb_class` has no `ciu` entry either, so the class gap exists in BOTH taxonomies. **Direction UNKNOWN and must be measured before any fix** — 370% may be above or below an Art. 152 look-through depending on the underlyings. ⚠ Do NOT change the C 07.00 admission gate to "fix" this: a CIU correctly leaves CR SA when it is IRB-treated (COREP Annex II ¶50 scopes that template to Chapter 2). **Effort: L** | Ref: PS1/26 Art. 147(2)(ea), 147(6)(a), 147A(i), Art. 152; CRR Art. 155(2)(c) · Ev: `src/rwa_calc/engine/equity/calculator.py:355-366`, `:750`; `src/rwa_calc/engine/entity_class_maps.py`

- **P6.37** [ ] **NEW (2026-05-28 audit — Polars forward-compat)** — 70 `.is_in([list-literal])` call sites across `src/rwa_calc/engine/` and `src/rwa_calc/reporting/` trigger the Polars `DeprecationWarning: "is_in with a collection of the same datatype is ambiguous and deprecated. Please use implode to return to previous behavior."` (observed on every CCR acceptance run, e.g. emitted from `engine/materialise.py:146` collect path). Representative sites: `reporting/corep/generator.py:2300,2348,2628,3980,4133,4156,4164,4166,4180`, `engine/classifier.py:313,316,1841,1910`, `engine/hierarchy.py:2494,2779`, `engine/equity/calculator.py:551`, `engine/ccf.py:300`. The warning is currently benign but a future Polars major will change `is_in([...])` semantics or remove the list-collection form. Sweep all 70 sites to the recommended form (wrap the literal in `pl.Series` / use `.implode()` per the polars guidance, or pin the comparison dtype). Pure forward-compat / code-hygiene — no current calculation or test impact (warnings only). **Effort: M** | Ref: https://github.com/pola-rs/polars/issues/22149; `grep -rn "\\.is_in(\\[" src/rwa_calc/` (70 hits)

- **P6.47** [ ] **NEW (2026-07-07 — durable root-cause fix behind the reconciliation loan-detail 500)** — `ResultsCache` writes every run to a **fixed** `<cache_dir>/last_results.parquet` (`src/rwa_calc/api/results_cache.py:130`), so a later `CreditRiskCalc.calculate()` / `.reconcile()` overwrites the very file that a still-registered `ReconciliationResponse` / `CalculationResponse` re-scans lazily on demand — a torn / stale read raising `polars ComputeError: parquet: File out of specification: The page header reported the wrong page size`. The Unreleased fix mitigated it two ways (reconciliation `loan_detail` + CSV/Excel exports now filter the **memoised** snapshot instead of re-scanning disk; all `ResultsCache` writes are now atomic via temp-file + `os.replace`), but the fixed-filename hazard remains for **any** consumer that re-scans on demand — results-page pagination (`get_page` / `scan_results`), a reconcile `component_reconciliation` / `class_allocation` re-scan, or a future export. Durable fix: give each run a **unique** results filename (`results_<run_id>.parquet`) so every registered response scans an **immutable snapshot**, and re-point `load_cached()` (which currently keys on the fixed name, `:163-187`) at a "latest" pointer — a small manifest JSON, or an atomically-updated copy/symlink. **Caveat:** needs a retention/GC policy (ref-count or TTL) so a snapshot is never deleted while a live response still references it. **Effort: M** | Ref: `src/rwa_calc/api/results_cache.py:130,163-187`, `src/rwa_calc/api/service.py:96-103,227`; `docs/appendix/changelog.md` [Unreleased] → Fixed

The Phase 9 entries are distinct new items but reuse the completed IDs. Documented here for a future renumbering pass.

#### Tier 6 — 2026-07-19 reporting-estate architecture review

> Six deliberate follow-ups from a 2026-07-19 architecture review of the COREP/Pillar III reporting estate (post-Phase-7 declarative-reporting completion — see the Architecture Migration cross-reference above). Not audit findings — the generators are functionally correct today; these are structural debt items surfaced by the review. Spot-checked against source: `reporting/corep/generator.py` (902 lines) and `reporting/pillar3/generator.py` (1012 lines) confirmed near-identical shape; `reporting/catalog.py::_TEMPLATES` confirmed as a 33-entry hand-maintained tuple (`catalog.py:132-178`); `reporting/corep/c08.py` confirmed 1482 lines; `_const`/`_Row`/`_null_empty_rows` duplication across c07/c08/c09 confirmed by grep; `Formula(..., fn=_const)` confirmed at 43 of 68 total `Formula(...)` call sites in `reporting/`; C34.01/02/04/08 and CCR1/2/3/8 confirmed present only as catalogue/column-list entries (`catalog.py`, `corep/templates.py`, `pillar3/templates.py`) with no per-template `cellspec` module (unlike c07/c08/c09/cr4-cr10/cms1/cms2/ov1, which each have one); `tests/fixtures/recon_ledger.py::with_reporting_ledger()` confirmed as a hand-listed column mapping independent of `contracts/edges.py::AGGREGATOR_EXIT_EDGE.conform()`; `api/rest.py` confirmed module-level `_RUNS`/`_TEMPLATE_BUNDLES` dicts (`:66,113`) with no persistence.

- **P6.48** [ ] **NEW (2026-07-19 architecture review)** **Registry-driven unified `TemplateGenerator`** — replace `COREPGenerator` + `Pillar3Generator` (two ~1000-line files with identical shape: frozen bundle, `generate`/`generate_from_lazyframe`/`export_to_excel` trio, per-field dispatch, sheet-writer helpers) with one generator driven by a template registry (id, family, framework-variant column resolver, spec-builder-or-imperative callable, axis kind, sheet naming). Bundle becomes id-keyed. Registry sourced from the cited rulepack `ReportingTemplateSet` (`resolve().reporting`; `rulebook/resolve.py`, `rulebook/model.py`), retiring the hand-maintained 33-entry `catalog.py::_TEMPLATES` list and closing the pack-blind gap noted in `reporting/metadata.py`. Export loop becomes data-driven off the registry. Number-neutral; goldens guard. **Sequence before any new template family is added.** High effort, high impact. **Effort: L** | Ref: `reporting/corep/generator.py`, `reporting/pillar3/generator.py`, `reporting/catalog.py:132-178`, `reporting/metadata.py`
- **P6.49** [ ] **NEW (2026-07-19 architecture review)** **Kernel-ise shared template helpers + two DSL primitives** — lift the copy-pasted `_prepare` discriminator ladders (defaulted/substitution/on-off-BS in c07/c08 and partially c09 — verify each ladder is identical before merging; keep c09's divergence explicit), `_null_empty_rows`, `_negate`/`_negate_deduction_cols`, `_const` (verbatim in `c07.py`/`c08.py`/`c09.py`), `_Row` (`c08.py`/`c09.py`), and the CR6/CR9 PD-band alloc+label+empty-band pattern into `reporting/kernel/`. Add a first-class `Constant` verb to `reporting/cellspec.py` (retires the `Formula(refs=(), fn=_const)` hack — confirmed 43 of 68 `Formula(...)` sites in `reporting/`, i.e. the majority) and a data-driven-row primitive (retires `_Row`). Also fix the `Formula` docstring's stale "~5 cells" claim in `cellspec.py`. Number-neutral. **Effort: M** | Ref: `reporting/corep/c07.py`, `reporting/corep/c08.py`, `reporting/corep/c09.py`, `reporting/cellspec.py`
- **P6.50** [ ] **NEW (2026-07-19 architecture review)** **Migrate C34.01/02/04/08 (COREP) and CCR1/2/3/8 (Pillar 3) onto the cellspec executor** — goldens FIRST per the recorded S8-pre rule (don't strangle an un-goldened template): author golden fixtures for each family, then port the imperative catalogue/column-list blocks (`reporting/catalog.py`, `reporting/corep/templates.py`, `reporting/pillar3/templates.py` — confirmed these 8 templates have no per-template `cellspec` module today, unlike c07/c08/c09/cr4-cr10/cms1/cms2/ov1) to per-template spec modules. This extends cell lineage (currently instrumented on a minority of the 33 catalogued templates) and any tie-out layer to those templates. **Depends on P6.49** — do not multiply existing duplication by porting against the un-kernelised helpers. **Effort: L** | Ref: `reporting/catalog.py:149-158,174-177`, `reporting/corep/templates.py`, `reporting/pillar3/templates.py`
- **P6.51** [ ] **NEW (2026-07-19 architecture review)** **Split `corep/c08.py` per sub-template** — 1482 lines / confirmed 107 `CellSpec(` sites covering C08.01-07 with materially different row logic; pure file split to `c08_01.py`..`c08_07.py` modules matching the one-module-per-template pattern used everywhere else in `reporting/corep/` and `reporting/pillar3/`. - **P6.54** [ ] **NEW (2026-09-08, adversarial review of P5.66) — the band-footing test's adequacy guard fires on the very defect the test exists to catch, so the failure message misdescribes the cause.** `tests/unit/reporting/corep/test_c07.py::TestSupportingFactors::test_c07_risk_weight_bands_foot_to_the_total_rwea` (`:620-644`) asserts `populated_bands.height >= 3` **before** the `for ref in ("0215", "0220")` footing loop. Both read the same quantity: a breakdown that silently drops a leg — the exact failure the docstring names — reduces the populated-band count, so the run stops on *"fewer than three populated bands would make this a single-row check"*, which reads as a fixture-adequacy complaint rather than a dropped-leg report. Cosmetic: the test still fails, and still fails for the right input. Fix by asserting the footing first and the adequacy after, or by counting bands on a column the defect cannot move (e.g. `row_ref` presence rather than `0220` non-null). Note this is the reverse of the ordering LESSONS C11 asks for on *fixture* adequacy, which is why it was written this way — the general rule holds; this one test's adequacy predicate happens to alias its subject. **Effort: S** | Ev: `tests/unit/reporting/corep/test_c07.py:620-644`

### Tier 8 — Counterparty Credit Risk (CCR) Integration (NEW 2026-05-21; reordered 2026-05-22 for first-batch execution)

**Status (2026-07-21 housekeeping):** the engine phases of this epic are **complete** and pruned to the Completed Items list — foundations + CCR-A1 (v0.2.11), the orchestrator-wiring quartet P8.19/P8.39/P8.53/P8.54, SA-CCR formula coverage (P8.15), CCR→IRB routing (P8.31), the CCR PD floor (P8.32), default-fund capital (P8.49), reporting (P8.50–P8.52), output floor (P8.55), BA-CVA (P8.60/P8.62/P8.63), and the acceptance-scenario families P8.42–P8.45. **Remaining:** P8.26 (nominally superseded by the now-closed P8.49 — verify no residual scope, then close), the test/citation residuals P8.46–P8.48, documentation P8.70–P8.74, and the Phase 10 v2.0 deferrals.

Major new epic adding Counterparty Credit Risk to the project. CCR is a separate-but-adjacent risk type to default credit risk: it produces the **EAD** for derivative and SFT (securities financing transaction) exposures, which then flows through the existing SA / IRB risk-weight machinery to produce the counterparty default-risk RWA. CVA (Credit Valuation Adjustment) capital is a related but separate framework and is scoped as a sub-epic (P8.60–P8.69).

**Pipeline shape:** the CCR stage sits between `Loader` and `Classifier` (or between `HierarchyResolver` and `Classifier`, TBD per P8.20). It consumes trade-level and netting-set-level inputs, produces exposure-level rows keyed by counterparty, and merges those rows into the existing `ExposureBundle` so downstream SA / IRB / Aggregator stages need minimal change.

**Execution ordering (2026-05-22 operator-approved):** the **first executable batch** (top of this tier) ships Phase 1 foundations + a single end-to-end vertical slice — **CCR-A1** (one unmargined IR swap → SA-CCR EAD → SA-RW → aggregator → pinned acceptance test). Source plan: `.claude/plans/can-we-start-to-linear-steele.md`. Within Tier 8 the first-batch items are listed at the top in dependency order; items marked **deferred to next batch after CCR-A1** are listed below them; items marked **deferred to v2.0** sit in Phase 10 alongside IMM.

**Scope decisions (operator-confirmed 2026-05-22):**
1. **Simplified SA-CCR (Art. 281) and OEM (Art. 282) — deferred to v2.0 / next-major-epic.** Not in initial epic. Sub-threshold portfolios fall through to full SA-CCR (always conservative). P8.21 and P8.22 reclassified accordingly (moved to Phase 10).
2. **CVA — BA-CVA only initially.** P8.60 (BA-CVA), P8.62 (hedges eligibility), P8.63 (aggregated CVA on bundle) stay in scope as the CVA sub-epic. **P8.61 (SA-CVA) deferred to v2.0** (moved to Phase 10).
3. **IMM — confirmed deferred to v2.0** (no change; P8.90 / P8.91 already there).
4. **SFTs — route through CCR with `risk_type=CCR_SFT`, reuse existing CRM haircut tables in `engine/crm/`.** No duplication of the supervisory haircut table. ~~The dual `risk_type` routing is already encompassed by existing tickets (no new P-code).~~ **Correction (2026-05-28):** the closing sentence proved wrong in practice. The current `engine/ccr/sa_ccr.py::compute_ead` runs the derivative `α·(RC+PFE)` formula on `transaction_type="sft"` rows regardless — only `engine/ccr/maturity_factor.py:121-127` branches on the SFT flag (5-BD margined MF floor). For a £60.7M debt-security SFT booked through the `trades` table this gives ~£4M EAD instead of the ~£65M `E·(1+HE)` the FCCM formula would produce. SFT-specific EAD wiring under SA-CCR per CRR Art. 271(2) was tracked under **P8.38** (since closed — the FCCM SFT branch shipped and was promoted to `engine/sft/fccm.py`).

**Cross-cutting blockers:**
- Trade-level fixtures are a step-change from the current exposure-level parquet shape. `fixture-builder` agent needs new builders before any acceptance scenario can be pinned (P8.40).
- The `output_floor.py` numerator must include SA-CCR-derived EAD × SA-RW for IMM firms (P8.55).

Non-QCCP default fund contributions: 1,250% RW (Art. 309).
